MEDICAL OS · MASTER ENGINEERING & IMPLEMENTATION SPECIFICATION
Medical OS V2.1 FINAL
Engineering Bible · Clinical Safety · Security · Data · AI · Interoperability · Compliance · Delivery
STATUS: ENGINEERING BASELINE AFTER FIVE REVIEW CYCLES V2.1 traduce el Product Freeze V2 a una secuencia ejecutable. Cada decisión importante debe poder rastrearse desde requisito V2 → arquitectura → código/configuración → prueba → evidencia → release. Este documento no autoriza afirmar certificación antes de completar evaluación de conformidad y evidencia aplicable.

Baseline date: 14 septiembre 2026 · Source baseline: Medical OS V2 FINAL

ENG-000 | ACTIVE | 0. Contrato entre V2 y V2.1
[ENG-000-R001] V2 es la especificación de producto y clínica. V2.1 no reabre objetivos por comodidad técnica: los transforma en ingeniería. Cuando una contradicción técnica obliga a modificar una intención V2, se genera Change Proposal con impacto clínico, UX, seguridad, compliance, datos y migración; no se resuelve silenciosamente en código.
Capa
V2
V2.1
Pregunta
Qué debe existir y por qué
Cómo se construye y demuestra
Unidad
Capability / workflow / outcome
Work package / contract / test / evidence
Aprobación
Product + Clinical
Architecture + Security + Clinical Safety + QA + Compliance
Cambio
Product Change Proposal
ADR + migration + test + traceability update
Salida
Product Freeze
Engineering Ready / Release Ready

ENG-001 | ACTIVE | 1. Resultado de los cinco ciclos de análisis
Ciclo
Pregunta de ataque
Mejora incorporada
1 — Arquitectura
¿Existen fronteras limpias o estamos construyendo módulos acoplados?
Platform/Clinical/Intelligence/Experience boundaries; domain contracts; dependency rules; ownership.
2 — Código y datos
¿Cada requisito puede aterrizar a entidad, API/evento, state machine, test y migration?
Data contracts, repository rules, events, APIs, idempotencia, schemas y migration strategy.
3 — Seguridad/compliance
¿Puede demostrarse confidencialidad, integridad, disponibilidad, trazabilidad y aislamiento?
Zero Trust, policy engine, evidence graph, NOM readiness, privacy and threat modeling.
4 — Clínica/IA
¿La automatización incrementa capacidad sin crear riesgo oculto?
Clinical safety cases, deterministic safety core, AI risk tiers, eval gates, human authority.
5 — Nanométrico
¿Quedó algún requisito V2 sin owner, gate o ruta de implementación?
Traceability matrix 0–113, orphan-requirement rule, release evidence manifest y no-unmapped-change gate.

FOUR-SIGNATURE RULE Toda feature clínica de impacto requiere aprobación conceptual mínima de Product/Workflow, Engineering, Clinical Safety y Security/Privacy. Si usa IA, suma AI Governance. Si afecta medicamentos/lab/imagen, suma el board especializado.

ENG-003 | ACTIVE | 3. Operating Model: de idea a producción
[ENG-003-R001] 1. Requirement ID nace o se referencia desde V2.
[ENG-003-R002] 2. Product/clinical acceptance criteria se congelan.
[ENG-003-R003] 3. Architecture owner asigna bounded context y ADR si aplica.
[ENG-003-R004] 4. Security/Privacy/Clinical Safety realizan impact classification.
[ENG-003-R005] 5. Data/API/event contracts se diseñan antes de UI final.
[ENG-003-R006] 6. Implementación en feature branch con environment aislado.
[ENG-003-R007] 7. Automated gates: static, unit, integration, clinical regression, security, AI evals si aplica.
[ENG-003-R008] 8. Preview clínico con datos sintéticos y UX review.
[ENG-003-R009] 9. Evidence manifest se genera automáticamente.
[ENG-003-R010] 10. CODEOWNERS approval.
[ENG-003-R011] 11. Staging/canary/feature flag.
[ENG-003-R012] 12. Production + post-deploy verification.
[ENG-003-R013] 13. Telemetry y safety signals se revisan.
[ENG-003-R014] 14. Change se cierra sólo con evidence links y acceptance demostrada.

4.1 Dependency law
UI → application service → authorization policy → domain service
   → repository/workflow → persistence/external adapter → audit/provenance

FORBIDDEN
UI → database table
UI → object storage direct
AI provider → signed clinical record
External webhook → clinical mutation without validation + idempotency
Domain A → Domain B tables directly without contract

ENG-006 | ACTIVE | 6. Engineering invariants
[ENG-006-R001] • TypeScript strict; external input always schema-validated.
[ENG-006-R002] • No clinical business logic inside React presentation components.
[ENG-006-R003] • No tenant identifier trusted from client; tenant context derives server-side.
[ENG-006-R004] • Internal identifiers non-predictable UUID/ULID; human folio is display/search identity, not authorization.
[ENG-006-R005] • Clinical state transitions use governed state machines; no arbitrary status strings.
[ENG-006-R006] • Dates/timezones explicit; clinical instant and local display separated.
[ENG-006-R007] • Quantities preserve value, unit and system; conversions preserve original.
[ENG-006-R008] • Signed content is immutable snapshot plus append-only amendment.
[ENG-006-R009] • Original imported document is immutable; derived data carries provenance.
[ENG-006-R010] • Every side effect triggered by event is idempotent or replay-safe.
[ENG-006-R011] • Safety-critical path does not depend exclusively on generative AI.
[ENG-006-R012] • No PHI in logs by default.
ENG-007 | ACTIVE | 7. Core type contracts
export interface ClinicalRef {
  type: string;
  id: string;
  version?: number;
}

export interface ProvenanceRef {
  sourceType: 'manual'|'document'|'integration'|'device'|'ai-assisted';
  sourceRef?: ClinicalRef;
  authorPractitionerId?: string;
  recordedAt: string;
  modelTraceId?: string;
}

export interface VersionedClinicalEntity {
  id: string;
  tenantId: string;
  patientId: string;
  version: number;
  lifecycleStatus: string;
  provenance: ProvenanceRef;
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
}

ENG-008 | ACTIVE | 8. Identity, tenancy and authorization
8.1 Identity & Tenancy
Dimensión
Especificación
Objetivo
Aislar organizaciones, facilities, usuarios y pacientes sin confianza implícita.
Owner
Identity + Security
Dependencias
IdP, DB foundation
Artefactos obligatorios
tenant/org/facility/user/practitioner/membership/role/policy/session/break-glass
Implementación
Policy enforcement server-side; repository-scoped tenant; optional RLS defense-in-depth; support sessions audited.
Pruebas
Cross-tenant IDOR/BOLA, role matrix, revoked session, break-glass expiry.
Seguridad / privacidad
MFA/passkeys for sensitive roles; session/device revocation; least privilege.
Clinical safety
Wrong-patient and unauthorized-access hazards reviewed.
Exit gate
Manipular IDs/rutas jamás devuelve recurso de otro tenant y audit explica decisión.

8.2 Policy model
Layer
Example
RBAC
physician, nurse, receptionist, billing, admin, auditor
ABAC
facility, purpose-of-use, sensitivity, shift, device/risk
ReBAC
care-team, referral, practitioner-patient relationship
Break-glass
reason + scope + expiry + enhanced audit + review

ENG-009 | ACTIVE | 9. Patient Identity & Master Patient Index
9.1 Patient identity
Dimensión
Especificación
Objetivo
Reducir duplicados, wrong-patient events y búsqueda lenta.
Owner
Patient Domain + Safety
Dependencias
Identity/Tenancy
Artefactos obligatorios
patient, identifier, contact_point, address, related_person, duplicate_candidate, merge_record
Implementación
Search normalized name/phone/folio/identifier; candidate scoring; merge is explicit governed workflow.
Pruebas
Duplicate fixtures, merge/unmerge policy tests, wrong-patient UI tests.
Seguridad / privacidad
Search result enumeration controls; purpose-limited fields; audit sensitive queries.
Clinical safety
Persistent patient header and identity confirmation at high-risk actions.
Exit gate
No silent merges; merge preserves source identities/provenance and is reversible according to policy.

STATE RECONSTRUCTION RULE El estado clínico debe poder reconstruirse “as-of” una fecha razonable usando objetos versionados, snapshots/eventos y provenance. No depender de la última nota narrativa como única verdad.

ENG-011 | ACTIVE | 11. Database conventions
[ENG-011-R001] • Postgres as primary relational truth.
[ENG-011-R002] • tenant_id on tenant-scoped tables; compound indexes begin with tenant_id where access pattern requires it.
[ENG-011-R003] • Foreign keys where lifecycle semantics allow them; soft lifecycle is not a substitute for audit.
[ENG-011-R004] • Optimistic concurrency for clinician-edited aggregates.
[ENG-011-R005] • Append-only audit/provenance tables logically separated.
[ENG-011-R006] • JSONB only for bounded extensibility/config/snapshot parameters, never giant chart.
[ENG-011-R007] • Migrations expand-and-contract; no destructive migration in same release that drops old path.
[ENG-011-R008] • Indexes justified by query plan and production-like synthetic volumes.
CREATE TABLE clinical_obligation (
  id uuid PRIMARY KEY,
  tenant_id uuid NOT NULL,
  patient_id uuid NOT NULL,
  source_type text NOT NULL,
  source_id uuid,
  reason text NOT NULL,
  priority text NOT NULL,
  owner_type text NOT NULL,
  owner_id uuid NOT NULL,
  due_window_start timestamptz,
  due_window_end timestamptz,
  state text NOT NULL,
  closure_criteria jsonb NOT NULL,
  escalation_policy_id uuid,
  version bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
  created_at timestamptz NOT NULL,
  closed_at timestamptz
);

ENG-012 | ACTIVE | 12. Patient State Engine
12.1 CurrentClinicalState
Dimensión
Especificación
Objetivo
Proveer una vista computable de lo que importa ahora sin obligar a releer notas.
Owner
Clinical Domain + Patient State
Dependencias
Clinical Data Foundation
Artefactos obligatorios
state projector, state schema, as-of query, cache policy, invalidation events
Implementación
Project from problems, meds, allergies, observations, results, obligations, recent changes, care gaps, inconsistencies.
Pruebas
Deterministic projection tests, historical as-of tests, invalidation tests.
Seguridad / privacidad
Minimum necessary panels; authorization at projection inputs and output.
Clinical safety
Never invent missing facts; unknown remains unknown.
Exit gate
Patient Workspace answers active problems, meds, allergies, recent changes, results and pending obligations within target budget.

interface CurrentClinicalState {
  patient: PatientIdentitySummary;
  activeProblems: ProblemSummary[];
  activeMedications: MedicationSummary[];
  allergies: AllergySummary[];
  recentVitals: ObservationSummary[];
  relevantResults: ResultSummary[];
  openObligations: ObligationSummary[];
  careGaps: CareGapSummary[];
  recentChanges: ChangeSummary[];
  inconsistencies: ConcordanceIssue[];
  safetyFlags: SafetyFlag[];
  computedAt: string;
  sourceVersions: string[];
}

ENG-013 | ACTIVE | 13. Patient Workspace implementation
13.1 Patient Workspace
Dimensión
Especificación
Objetivo
Convertir CurrentClinicalState en la superficie longitudinal principal.
Owner
Experience + Patient State
Dependencias
Patient State Engine, Design System
Artefactos obligatorios
persistent header, summary panels, timeline, problems, meds, studies, notes, preventive, documents, intelligence rail
Implementación
Panel queries independent; progressive loading; preserve encounter context; command palette; deep-linkable subviews.
Pruebas
E2E time-to-answer, navigation-preservation, permission/error/loading states, mobile capability tests.
Seguridad / privacidad
Field-level/surface-level access; no sensitive side-panel leakage.
Clinical safety
Critical allergies/risks are visible without color-only semantics.
Exit gate
Clinician can answer six state questions in <10–15s target on benchmark chart.

ENG-014 | ACTIVE | 14. Encounter Engine
type EncounterState =
  | 'planned' | 'arrived' | 'in_progress' | 'ready_to_sign'
  | 'signed' | 'amended' | 'closed' | 'cancelled';

14.1 Encounter Workspace
Dimensión
Especificación
Objetivo
Completar consulta habitual sin saltos entre módulos.
Owner
Encounter + UX + Clinical Board
Dependencias
Patient State, history, notes, meds, orders
Artefactos obligatorios
state machine, drafts, autosave, sections, checkpoint, signature, amendment
Implementación
One workspace coordinates personal context→vitals→HPI→studies→exam→assessment→plan→Rx→sign.
Pruebas
State transition tests, autosave conflict, offline/retry, sign/amend E2E.
Seguridad / privacidad
Authorization per mutation; signed snapshot integrity.
Clinical safety
Critical unresolved blockers surface before sign; overrides captured.
Exit gate
Normal encounter completes end-to-end without losing context.

ENG-015 | ACTIVE | 15. Adaptive Clinical History
interface ClinicalQuestionDefinition {
  id:string; version:string; conceptCode:string; label:LocalizedText;
  responseType:'boolean'|'number'|'choice'|'date'|'text'|'coded';
  applicability:RuleExpression; dependencies:string[];
  repeatPolicy:'once'|'confirm_each_encounter'|'time_window'|'on_change';
  clinicalImpact:Array<'safety'|'differential'|'testing'|'treatment'|'followup'>;
  evidence?:EvidenceRef[];
}

[ENG-015-R001] • Store explicit unknown/not-asked/negative/positive/not-applicable.
[ENG-015-R002] • Previous answers reused according to repeatPolicy; never silently treat stale answer as current.
[ENG-015-R003] • Narrative renderer references structured answers rather than duplicating truth.
[ENG-015-R004] • Clinician can always document free text with provenance.
[ENG-015-R005] • Question graph version recorded per encounter.
ENG-016 | ACTIVE | 16. Vitals & Observation architecture
[ENG-016-R001] • Observation = value/coded result + unit/system + effective time + source/device + performer + quality/context.
[ENG-016-R002] • BP supports components, position/cuff/context when captured.
[ENG-016-R003] • Pediatric values evaluate against age/context-specific content packages.
[ENG-016-R004] • Repeat readings in same encounter preserved, not overwritten.
[ENG-016-R005] • UI shows current + delta + baseline + trend; abnormality is contextual, not hardcoded in component.
[ENG-016-R006] • Glucometry/pain/waist/Glasgow etc. are context-enabled extensions, not mandatory noise.
ENG-017 | ACTIVE | 17. Physical Examination OS
[ENG-017-R001] • ExamFinding is structured concept where useful plus narrative region.
[ENG-017-R002] • Focused exam suggestions come from Clinical Knowledge Package and chief-complaint context.
[ENG-017-R003] • Absence of a documented exam ≠ normal.
[ENG-017-R004] • Exam templates are specialty/content versions, not static frontend forms.
[ENG-017-R005] • System records clinician-observed vs imported/AI-extracted provenance distinctly.
ENG-018 | ACTIVE | 18. Assessment, hypotheses and Problem Lifecycle
interface DiagnosticHypothesis {
  id:string; encounterId:string; concept?:CodeableConcept; label:string;
  status:'considering'|'favored'|'less-likely'|'ruled-out'|'promoted-to-problem';
  evidenceFor:ClinicalRef[]; evidenceAgainst:ClinicalRef[]; missingEvidence:ClinicalRef[];
  clinicianConfidence?:'low'|'moderate'|'high';
}

[ENG-018-R001] • Diagnosis/problem certainty separate from lifecycle state.
[ENG-018-R002] • Assessment can contain unresolved hypotheses without polluting chronic problem list.
[ENG-018-R003] • Promotion to problem is explicit clinician action.
[ENG-018-R004] • Reasoning Engine may organize evidence but does not assign authoritative diagnosis.
ENG-019 | ACTIVE | 19. Clinical Decision Checkpoint
[ENG-019-R001] Before sign, the checkpoint composes unresolved critical safety flags, missing high-impact information, unsigned orders, unowned obligations, medication overrides, unreviewed newly-uploaded critical evidence and contradictions. It is not a generic “complete all fields” screen.
ENG-020 | ACTIVE | 20. Notes, legal snapshot and amendment
signEncounter():
  validate required legal/clinical fields
  evaluate critical safety gate
  freeze referenced structured versions
  render legal narrative snapshot
  compute snapshot hash
  persist signature metadata
  append audit/provenance
  publish EncounterSigned
  reject destructive post-sign update

ENG-021 | ACTIVE | 21. Medication OS
21.1 Medication lifecycle
Dimensión
Especificación
Objetivo
Conectar indicación, prescripción, inicio, respuesta, cambios, vigilancia y suspensión.
Owner
Medication Safety + Clinical Domain
Dependencias
Problems, observations, terminology
Artefactos obligatorios
medication concept/presentation, statement, request, reconciliation, monitoring, adverse event hooks
Implementación
Separate catalog concept from prescription order; safety evaluator before sign; formula inputs preserved.
Pruebas
Dose/unit/route/frequency boundaries, allergy, duplicate, interaction, renal/hepatic/pregnancy contexts where governed.
Seguridad / privacidad
Override reason + rule version; no hidden auto-renew.
Clinical safety
Safety rules deterministic where high impact; evidence/version visible.
Exit gate
Every active medication can answer why, who, dose, status, response/monitoring if applicable.

21.2 Pediatric dosing
CalculatedDose {
  weightValue; weightUnit; weightObservedAt;
  formulaId; formulaVersion; dosePerKg;
  calculatedDose; cappedDose?;
  prescribedDose; clinicianConfirmedAt;
}

[ENG-021-R001] Calculated dose and prescribed dose remain distinct. Stale weight triggers explicit warning according to governed policy; the calculator never signs the prescription.
ENG-022 | ACTIVE | 22. Prescription Studio
[ENG-022-R001] • Visual canvas is a template editor; legal prescription data is generated server-side from signed clinical objects.
[ENG-022-R002] • Country Pack locks mandatory fields and legal blocks.
[ENG-022-R003] • Templates versioned; each emitted Rx stores template version/render hash.
[ENG-022-R004] • QR contains verification token/reference, not sensitive PHI in clear.
[ENG-022-R005] • Print/PDF/email/secure patient link share the same signed source object.
ENG-023 | ACTIVE | 23. Orders, diagnostics and referrals
ServiceRequest state: draft → active → scheduled/collected? → completed | cancelled | not-performed
Diagnostic workflow: request → result_received → review_required → acknowledged → actioned → informed? → closed

[ENG-023-R001] • Orders create obligations when future review/action is expected.
[ENG-023-R002] • Result received and result reviewed are different facts.
[ENG-023-R003] • Cancellation/not-performed requires reason; no disappearing order.
[ENG-023-R004] • Referrals have send/accept/appointment/report/closure states where integrations allow.
ENG-024 | ACTIVE | 24. Clinical Obligation Engine
24.1 Obligation engine
Dimensión
Especificación
Objetivo
Generalizar closed-loop a todo futuro clínicamente importante.
Owner
Workflow + Clinical Safety
Dependencias
Orders, meds, findings, guidelines
Artefactos obligatorios
obligation entity, owner, due window, escalation, closure criteria, timers, inbox views
Implementación
Durable idempotent workflows; explicit owner; backup/escalation policy by priority.
Pruebas
Timer replay, duplicate event, owner unavailable, overdue escalation, closure evidence.
Seguridad / privacidad
No public notification of sensitive reason; least necessary content.
Clinical safety
Critical obligations have deterministic escalation independent of LLM.
Exit gate
No obligation can become invisible without terminal state and evidence/reason.

ENG-025 | ACTIVE | 25. Result Inbox
[ENG-025-R001] • Queues: critical/important/routine, unreviewed, awaiting action, patient communication, overdue.
[ENG-025-R002] • Acknowledgement is clinician/team action with timestamp; not inferred from opening screen.
[ENG-025-R003] • Critical result policy separately governed and tested.
[ENG-025-R004] • Bulk actions forbidden where patient-context safety would be reduced.
[ENG-025-R005] • Escalation and closure metrics feed safety dashboard.
ENG-026 | ACTIVE | 26. Document Intelligence Pipeline
upload → quarantine → MIME/content validation → malware scan → hash
→ immutable private object → classification → OCR/parser/model extraction
→ candidate structured data + source anchors + confidence
→ human validation / trusted integration validation
→ publish observations/report → longitudinal linking → intelligence/obligation

[ENG-026-R001] • Original never replaced by normalized output.
[ENG-026-R002] • Extracted value carries page/region/source pointer where available.
[ENG-026-R003] • Prompt injection content treated as untrusted data.
[ENG-026-R004] • Parser/model cannot execute instructions found in document.
[ENG-026-R005] • PHI absent from routine ingestion logs.
ENG-027 | ACTIVE | 27. Laboratory OS
[ENG-027-R001] • Preserve analyte, original name/code, canonical mapping, value, unit, reference interval, method/lab, flags, specimen and status.
[ENG-027-R002] • Unit normalization never discards original.
[ENG-027-R003] • Delta/trend analysis distinguishes analytic change from clinical interpretation.
[ENG-027-R004] • Microbiology: specimen, organism, susceptibility, method, preliminary/final.
[ENG-027-R005] • Parasitology: specimen, technique, organism/stage and semi-quantitative results where provided.
[ENG-027-R006] • AI layer separates abnormality → trend → correlation → considerations → limitations; no diagnosis by formatting.
ENG-028 | ACTIVE | 28. Imaging OS
[ENG-028-R001] • DICOM/DICOMweb adapter and PACS integration behind boundary.
[ENG-028-R002] • ImagingStudyRef, Report, Finding, Measurement, FollowUpRecommendation.
[ENG-028-R003] • Longitudinal comparison only when measurement semantics compatible.
[ENG-028-R004] • Actionable validated finding can create obligation.
[ENG-028-R005] • AI imaging intended-use features require regulatory classification and separate validation/QMS path if applicable.
ENG-029 | ACTIVE | 29. Pathology OS
[ENG-029-R001] • Specimen and procedure context first-class.
[ENG-029-R002] • Report status preliminary/final/amended preserved.
[ENG-029-R003] • Structured findings/margins/biomarkers only where source supports them.
[ENG-029-R004] • Pathology actionable recommendation becomes obligation after appropriate validation.
ENG-030 | ACTIVE | 30. Clinical Knowledge Architecture
ClinicalKnowledgePackage {
  id; version; specialty; countryScopes[]; reviewers[]; effectiveDate; sources[];
  applicability; questions[]; redFlags[]; focusedExam[]; differentialSupports[];
  orderConsiderations[]; medicationConsiderations[]; calculators[];
  followUpRules[]; careGaps[]; safetyNet[]; regressionCases[];
}

[ENG-030-R001] • Content has separate release/version lifecycle from application code.
[ENG-030-R002] • Every high-impact rule has source/effective date/reviewer/test cases.
[ENG-030-R003] • Frontend never embeds authoritative red-flag logic in JSX.
[ENG-030-R004] • Expired/superseded content remains reproducible for historical encounter review.
ENG-031 | ACTIVE | 31. Clinical Intelligence Engine contracts
Engine
Output
Patient State
current computable state
Reasoning
hypothesis/evidence organization
Omission
potential missing high-impact data/actions
Safety
deterministic hazards and governed warnings
Trajectory
baseline-relative change
Care Gap
prevention/monitoring
Obligation
future closure
Knowledge
applicability/evidence/version
Concordance
contradictions and reconciliation

interface ClinicalSuggestion {
  suggestionId:string; kind:'info'|'consider'|'important'|'critical';
  title:string; rationale:string; patientDataRefs:ClinicalRef[]; evidenceRefs:EvidenceRef[];
  action?:SuggestedAction; suppressible:boolean; expiresWhen?:RuleExpression;
  contentVersion:string; generatedAt:string;
}

ENG-032 | ACTIVE | 32. Omission, Concordance and Trajectory
[ENG-032-R001] • Omission Engine asks: what missing fact/action could materially change safety, differential, test, treatment or follow-up?
[ENG-032-R002] • Concordance Engine flags contradictions (allergy vs Rx, diagnosis vs problem state, impossible age/context, duplicated therapies, note vs structured record) for reconciliation.
[ENG-032-R003] • Trajectory Engine compares with patient baseline and detects sustained/progressive changes; it never declares causality.
[ENG-032-R004] • All engines support suppression/expiration and measure usefulness to control alert fatigue.
ENG-033 | ACTIVE | 33. Care Gap and Chronic Disease Spaces
[ENG-033-R001] Care gaps derive from governed knowledge + patient context and appear as opportunities, not interruption spam. Chronic Disease Space composes problem, goals, measurements, meds, surveillance, obligations, results and trajectory around a living condition workspace.
ENG-034 | ACTIVE | 34. AI Gateway — mandatory boundary
Clinical package → AiTaskRequest → ai-gateway
  → policy / context minimization / redaction
  → provider or self-hosted router
  → structured-output validator
  → grounding/provenance check
  → eval/telemetry hooks
  → clinician-facing proposal

NO package imports OpenAI/Anthropic/Google/local SDK directly.

interface AiTaskResult<T> {
  output:T; provider:string; model:string; modelVersion?:string;
  policyHash:string; citations:ClinicalRef[]; uncertainty?:string[];
  traceId:string; generatedAt:string;
}

ENG-035 | ACTIVE | 35. AI Risk Tiers and release gates
Tier
Examples
Gate
A — assistive low impact
rewrite, patient-friendly explanation from approved content
schema + factuality + privacy
B — extraction
document/lab extraction
field precision/recall + human validation policy
C — clinical synthesis
pre-visit summary, longitudinal changes
grounding + omission + unsupported-claim eval
D — reasoning support
differential/evidence considerations
clinical safety set + abstention + physician review + shadow mode
E — potential diagnostic/therapeutic intended use
high-impact prediction/recommendation
regulatory classification, validation/QMS path before enablement

ENG-036 | ACTIVE | 36. AI evaluation framework
[ENG-036-R001] • Datasets stratified by task, language, age/context, specialty, ambiguity, urgency and adversarial inputs.
[ENG-036-R002] • Measure factuality, unsupported claims, omission, extraction precision/recall, negation/temporality, unsafe suggestion, calibration/abstention.
[ENG-036-R003] • Model/prompt/policy changes are versioned changes requiring eval.
[ENG-036-R004] • Shadow-mode comparison before high-impact clinician-facing activation.
[ENG-036-R005] • No chain-of-thought storage; store inputs policy/context refs, output, model/version, decision/accept-edit-reject where appropriate.
ENG-037 | ACTIVE | 37. Patient Link, Portal and communications
[ENG-037-R001] • Transactional ShareGrant with scope, expiration, max uses, optional OTP, revocation.
[ENG-037-R002] • Persistent patient portal has real identity/session, not permanent anonymous token.
[ENG-037-R003] • SMS/WhatsApp/email adapters send minimum necessary notification; sensitive content fetched after secure access.
[ENG-037-R004] • Communication events link to encounter/obligation/result when clinically relevant.
[ENG-037-R005] • Consent and channel preferences explicit and versioned.
ENG-038 | ACTIVE | 38. Scheduling, rooming and operations
[ENG-038-R001] • Facility timezone explicit; recurrence and daylight-saving handled by library/standards, not hand math.
[ENG-038-R002] • Appointment→check-in→rooming→encounter without duplicate patient.
[ENG-038-R003] • Double-booking prevention uses constraints/transactions where applicable.
[ENG-038-R004] • Billing/inventory use separate domains and ledgers; never rewrite clinical truth.
[ENG-038-R005] • Operational status may reference encounter but cannot mutate signed clinical content.
ENG-039 | ACTIVE | 39. Interoperability architecture
[ENG-039-R001] • Internal domain is not FHIR-shaped by force; FHIR facade/mapping is boundary.
[ENG-039-R002] • FHIR R5 is current published HL7 version, but implementation profiles/version are negotiated per integration/country; preserve compatibility strategy.
[ENG-039-R003] • Terminology service stores canonical + original codes.
[ENG-039-R004] • HL7 v2 adapters isolate transport, mapping and business logic.
[ENG-039-R005] • DICOM/DICOMweb for imaging.
[ENG-039-R006] • SMART App Launch considered for app integrations where profile/ecosystem requires it.
[ENG-039-R007] • Retry/dead-letter/reconciliation for external exchanges; replayability without excessive PHI logs.

[ENG-041-R001] México es Country Pack 001. International-ready significa core global + reglas locales versionadas; no significa traducir etiquetas ni hardcodear CURP en Patient universal.
ENG-042 | ACTIVE | 42. Security Architecture
[ENG-042-R001] • Zero Trust: no implicit trust by network location; authenticate/authorize subject and device/session as appropriate.
[ENG-042-R002] • MFA/passkeys for sensitive roles; short-lived credentials and rotation for services.
[ENG-042-R003] • Deny-by-default policy; step-up for export/high-risk admin.
[ENG-042-R004] • Private object storage; signed short-lived URLs/proxy.
[ENG-042-R005] • WAF/rate limiting, secure headers, CSP appropriate to app, CSRF/session protections.
[ENG-042-R006] • Secrets in managed secret store; never repo/env examples with real values.
[ENG-042-R007] • SAST, SCA, secret scan, IaC/config scan, DAST/API security tests.
[ENG-042-R008] • Dependency updates automated but gated by tests.
[ENG-042-R009] • Security events centralized; incident response and evidence retention.
42.1 Threat model catalog
Threat
Primary controls
Cross-tenant IDOR
server tenant context + scoped repos + tests + RLS defense
Wrong patient
persistent header + high-risk confirmation + duplicate controls
Session theft
MFA/passkeys + rotation/revocation + risk signals
PHI logs
allowlisted structured logging + scanners
Malicious upload
quarantine + AV + parser isolation
Prompt injection
document text untrusted; tool policy prevents execution
Webhook replay
signature/timestamp/idempotency
Insider abuse
least privilege + audit + anomaly review
Ransomware/data loss
PITR + immutable/segregated backup strategy + restore drills
Supply chain
SBOM + signed/reproducible build strategy where feasible + dependency policy

ENG-043 | ACTIVE | 43. Privacy Engineering
[ENG-043-R001] • Purpose-of-use and minimum necessary are explicit design inputs.
[ENG-043-R002] • Consent object records scope, purpose, actor, dates, withdrawal/supersession.
[ENG-043-R003] • Data subject workflows are jurisdiction-aware and do not bypass legal retention duties.
[ENG-043-R004] • Analytics defaults to de-identified/aggregated where possible; PHI analytics requires purpose and access policy.
[ENG-043-R005] • AI provider routing checks data policy and tenant configuration.
[ENG-043-R006] • Export, bulk download and support access are high-risk audited operations.
ENG-044 | ACTIVE | 44. México Compliance-as-Code baseline
[ENG-044-R001] V2.1 uses an applicability register rather than claiming “all NOMs”. Official sources checked for this baseline list NOM-004-SSA3-2012 and NOM-024-SSA3-2012 as applicable foundational references for clinical record/SIRES scope; LFPDPPP current text is maintained from Cámara de Diputados. NOM-151-SCFI-2016 and NOM-241-SSA1-2025 are evaluated when their scope/intended use applies.
Framework
Engineering consequence
NOM-004-SSA3-2012
Clinical note/document requirements, identity, integrity, content/retention mapping.
NOM-024-SSA3-2012
SIRES exchange/security/conformance evidence; structured/unalterable electronic record controls and certification readiness.
LFPDPPP
Sensitive personal data, purpose/consent, rights, security, transfers, breach/privacy governance.
NOM-151-SCFI-2016
Evaluate conservation/digitalization/message-data evidence when applicable.
NOM-241-SSA1-2025
If a feature qualifies as software as medical device, branch into applicable quality/regulatory validation path.

  → code/config artifact
  → verification test

  → owner
  → release version
  → review/expiry

ENG-045 | ACTIVE | 45. Clinical Safety Management System
[ENG-045-R001] • Hazard log per feature/workflow; safety case for high-impact functions.
[ENG-045-R002] • Severity/likelihood/residual-risk model defined by clinical board and quality governance.
[ENG-045-R003] • Safety control types: preventive, detective, recovery.
[ENG-045-R004] • Wrong-patient, result follow-up, ordering, medication, alert fatigue, duplicate patient and downtime are mandatory hazard families.
[ENG-045-R005] • Safety signal telemetry after release; rollback/disable via feature flag when appropriate.
ENG-046 | ACTIVE | 46. Human factors and alert governance
[ENG-046-R001] • Alert must have clinical purpose, severity, action and expiration/suppression logic.
[ENG-046-R002] • Critical is rare and blocks only when justified; Important requires explicit review/override; Consider is non-interruptive; Info is ambient.
[ENG-046-R003] • Measure display→action/override/dismiss, downstream outcome proxy and burden.
[ENG-046-R004] • Alert Governance Board reviews high-volume or low-value alerts.
[ENG-046-R005] • Color never sole carrier of meaning.

ENG-049 | ACTIVE | 49. Durable workflow runtime
[ENG-049-R001] • Timers survive deploy/restart.
[ENG-049-R002] • Workflow versioning/migration strategy defined before long-running patient obligations.
[ENG-049-R003] • Retries exponential/backoff with dead-letter/quarantine and operator visibility.
[ENG-049-R004] • Critical overdue workflows escalate deterministically.
[ENG-049-R005] • Manual repair actions audited and idempotent.
ENG-050 | ACTIVE | 50. Search architecture
[ENG-050-R001] • Global command/search bar supports patient name, folio, phone/email/identifier according to permissions.
[ENG-050-R002] • Search indices are tenant-scoped; no unauthenticated enumeration.
[ENG-050-R003] • Clinical search within patient distinguishes exact structured data from full-text notes/documents.
[ENG-050-R004] • Search results visually preserve patient identity/context to reduce wrong-patient selection.
[ENG-050-R005] • Query analytics never log raw sensitive query by default.

ENG-052 | ACTIVE | 52. Accessibility baseline

ENG-059 | ACTIVE | 59. Database migration policy
[ENG-059-R001] • Preview every migration against representative synthetic data volume.
[ENG-059-R002] • Expand-and-contract for rename/drop.
[ENG-059-R003] • Long locks estimated and mitigated.
[ENG-059-R004] • Application backward compatibility during progressive deploy.
[ENG-059-R005] • Audit/provenance schema changes require Security/Compliance review.
[ENG-059-R006] • Migration has rollback or restore strategy; irreversible data transform gets rehearsal and checksums.
ENG-060 | ACTIVE | 60. Release train and feature flags
[ENG-060-R001] • Separate application release from clinical-content release when architecture supports it.
[ENG-060-R002] • High-risk intelligence releases default shadow/canary before general availability.
[ENG-060-R003] • Feature flag has owner, purpose, expiry/cleanup date; no permanent flag cemetery.
[ENG-060-R004] • Emergency disable path for AI/clinical content not required for core chart access.
[ENG-060-R005] • Release manifest includes app commit, DB migration set, knowledge versions, country pack version, AI policy/models and evidence bundle IDs.

ENG-068 | ACTIVE | 68. Dependency graph / no-parallelism mistakes
[ENG-068-R001] Parallel work is allowed only after contracts stabilize. Examples: Prescription Studio UI can prototype early but cannot finalize legal rendering before Country Pack/Rx data contract; AI summary can prototype on fixtures but cannot enter clinical workflow before Patient State/provenance/eval harness; Result Inbox cannot ship before owner/acknowledgement/obligation semantics.

ENG-071 | ACTIVE | 71. Specialty implementation strategy
[ENG-071-R001] • No specialty fork.
[ENG-071-R002] • Specialty Pack extends clinical content, screens only when workflow materially differs.
[ENG-071-R003] • Life-stage context (neonatal/pediatric/adult/geriatric/pregnancy) can activate cross-specialty rules.
[ENG-071-R004] • Each pack has reviewers, sources, regression suite, applicability, safety case and version.
[ENG-071-R005] • Intern/resident layer is assistance configuration, not separate chart truth.
ENG-072 | ACTIVE | 72. Laboratory/QBP engineering board gates
[ENG-072-R001] • Structured result model reviewed for chemistry/hematology/microbiology/parasitology.
[ENG-072-R002] • Specimen/method/status/quality flags retained.
[ENG-072-R003] • Canonical mappings do not erase original lab semantics.
[ENG-072-R004] • Reference intervals are source/context aware.
[ENG-072-R005] • Critical-value logic governed separately from generative interpretation.
[ENG-072-R006] • Integration fixtures include preliminary/final/amended and corrected result scenarios.
ENG-073 | ACTIVE | 73. Imaging engineering board gates
[ENG-073-R001] • DICOM identifiers and external PACS references separated from human folios.
[ENG-073-R002] • DICOMweb adapter test fixtures.
[ENG-073-R003] • Report amendments preserved.
[ENG-073-R004] • Finding measurement units/axes and comparison semantics explicit.
[ENG-073-R005] • Follow-up recommendation creates obligation only through validated rule/clinician workflow.
[ENG-073-R006] • Any diagnostic AI feature gets intended-use/regulatory review before code path activation.
ENG-074 | ACTIVE | 74. Medication safety board gates
[ENG-074-R001] • Medication concept/presentation/order not conflated.
[ENG-074-R002] • Dose unit normalization tested.
[ENG-074-R003] • Pediatric formula/version/weight date persisted.
[ENG-074-R004] • Allergy/interaction/duplicate therapy logic versioned.
[ENG-074-R005] • Renal/hepatic/pregnancy rules only from governed content.
[ENG-074-R006] • Override reasons and high-risk override telemetry available.
ENG-075 | ACTIVE | 75. Security release gates
[ENG-075-R001] • No known critical/high vulnerability accepted without documented risk decision and compensating controls.
[ENG-075-R002] • Cross-tenant automated suite passes.
[ENG-075-R003] • Secrets scan clean.
[ENG-075-R004] • Auth/session/break-glass tests pass.
[ENG-075-R005] • Upload and SSRF/injection classes assessed where applicable.
[ENG-075-R006] • External pentest before clinical production and after major attack-surface change per security program.
[ENG-075-R007] • Incident runbook and contacts tested.
ENG-076 | ACTIVE | 76. Clinical production gates
[ENG-076-R001] • Synthetic end-to-end validation complete.
[ENG-076-R002] • Usability testing with target clinicians.
[ENG-076-R003] • Clinical regression baseline signed off.
[ENG-076-R004] • SAFER-style self-assessment performed for relevant areas.
[ENG-076-R005] • Compliance applicability matrix reviewed.
[ENG-076-R006] • Security assessment/pentest complete.
[ENG-076-R007] • Restore drill successful.
[ENG-076-R008] • Pilot rollback and feature-disable path tested.
[ENG-076-R009] • Joint go-live sign-off: Product, Clinical Safety, Engineering, QA, Security, Privacy/Compliance.
ENG-077 | ACTIVE | 77. Metrics that prove function, not marketing
Dimension
Core metrics
Clinical cognition
time-to-clinical-answer, unresolved critical findings
Continuity
closed-loop rate, overdue obligations, lost follow-up
Documentation
time-to-sign, after-hours charting, duplicate entry
Safety
critical alert precision, medication overrides, wrong-patient near misses
AI
accept/edit/reject, unsupported claim rate, omission rate, abstention
UX
task time, clicks, reversals, abandonment
Reliability
p95 critical flows, workflow failures, restore success
Security
cross-tenant pass, vuln aging, break-glass review
Adoption
active clinicians, completed encounters, retention

ENG-078 | ACTIVE | 78. Anti-pattern blacklist
[ENG-078-R001] • Build CRUD first and “add medicine later”.
[ENG-078-R002] • Store chart as giant JSON.
[ENG-078-R003] • Authorize in frontend.
[ENG-078-R004] • Use production DB in preview.
[ENG-078-R005] • Public PHI storage.
[ENG-078-R006] • Destructive signed-note edits.
[ENG-078-R007] • Simple cron as sole critical follow-up guarantee.
[ENG-078-R008] • Clinical rules in JSX/prompts.
[ENG-078-R009] • Send whole chart to AI by convenience.
[ENG-078-R010] • One AI provider hard dependency.
[ENG-078-R011] • FHIR as internal UX/domain straightjacket.
[ENG-078-R012] • Permanent public patient links.
[ENG-078-R013] • Alerts without owner/action/suppression/usefulness metrics.
[ENG-078-R014] • Certification claims without evidence and evaluation.
[ENG-078-R015] • Autonomous diagnosis/prescribing by accidental feature creep.

ENG-085 | ACTIVE | 85. Migration from legacy/paper
[ENG-085-R001] • Import is staged, never direct truth injection.
[ENG-085-R002] • Every imported artifact has source/system/date and confidence/validation state.
[ENG-085-R003] • Legacy free text remains available while structured extraction is candidate until validated.
[ENG-085-R004] • Patient matching before import has manual resolution for ambiguity.
[ENG-085-R005] • Migration rehearsal includes counts/checksums/sampling and rollback.
[ENG-085-R006] • Do not fabricate missing provenance from legacy data.
ENG-086 | ACTIVE | 86. Multi-facility and enterprise scaling
[ENG-086-R001] • Facility hierarchy and memberships from start.
[ENG-086-R002] • Policy scope can bind facility/team/purpose without forking schema.
[ENG-086-R003] • Enterprise dedicated tenancy/storage option should not change clinical-domain contracts.
[ENG-086-R004] • SSO/SCIM or enterprise IAM adapters added behind identity boundary when demanded.
[ENG-086-R005] • Small-practice UX remains default; enterprise controls live in admin, not cluttering encounter.
ENG-087 | ACTIVE | 87. Data retention, deletion and legal hold architecture
[ENG-087-R001] Retention rules are Country Pack/compliance policy, not hardcoded delete jobs. Clinical/legal records may have retention obligations that supersede a simple user deletion request; privacy workflows therefore produce assessed actions, export/access/correction workflows and legal-hold-aware disposition where applicable.
ENG-088 | ACTIVE | 88. Support and operations access
[ENG-088-R001] • No shared “superadmin” credentials.
[ENG-088-R002] • Support access is scoped, reasoned, time-bounded and audited.
[ENG-088-R003] • Default support view minimizes PHI; escalation to content access requires explicit policy.
[ENG-088-R004] • Production data fixes use controlled runbook/tooling; direct ad-hoc SQL is exceptional and evidenced.
ENG-089 | ACTIVE | 89. Incident response
Severity
Example
Response
SEV0
patient safety/security catastrophic ongoing
immediate command + disable/contain + clinical leadership
SEV1
major clinical workflow/security impact
rapid incident command
SEV2
degraded non-critical function
same-day ownership
SEV3
minor/local defect
normal queue

[ENG-089-R001] Security incidents and clinical safety incidents may overlap; runbooks define when to involve privacy, clinical leadership, vendors and regulatory/legal review.
ENG-090 | ACTIVE | 90. Documentation architecture
[ENG-090-R001] • docs/adr: why architecture decisions exist.
[ENG-090-R002] • docs/product: V2 requirement excerpts/links and change proposals.
[ENG-090-R003] • docs/clinical-safety: hazards/safety cases.
[ENG-090-R004] • docs/threat-models: data flows/abuse cases.
[ENG-090-R005] • docs/compliance: applicability/control/evidence mappings.
[ENG-090-R006] • docs/runbooks: operational/recovery procedures.
[ENG-090-R007] • Generated docs never replace reviewed source-of-truth artifacts without ownership.

ENG-092 | ACTIVE | 92. Product analytics without surveillance
[ENG-092-R001] • Measure workflow friction using event names/IDs, not raw clinical content.
[ENG-092-R002] • No session replay capturing PHI unless explicitly engineered, justified and protected; default is no.
[ENG-092-R003] • Clinical safety metrics use structured event references and aggregate reporting.
[ENG-092-R004] • Tenant benchmarking requires privacy/contract policy and appropriate aggregation.
ENG-093 | ACTIVE | 93. Change management after V2.1 freeze
[ENG-093-R001] 1. Open RFC/Change Proposal.
[ENG-093-R002] 2. Link affected V2/V2.1 IDs.
[ENG-093-R003] 3. Assess clinical/UX/security/privacy/regulatory/data/migration impact.
[ENG-093-R004] 4. Approve/reject by relevant owners.
[ENG-093-R005] 5. Create/modify ADR if architecture changes.
[ENG-093-R006] 6. Update traceability/evidence/test expectations before merge.
[ENG-093-R007] 7. Version release notes and content/model changes.

ENG-095 | ACTIVE | 95. Final engineering freeze declaration
V2.1 FREEZE CONDITION V2.1 becomes the engineering baseline when: all V2 requirements remain mapped; foundational ADRs are approved; compliance applicability has owners; domain contracts for the golden vertical slice are stable; threat/clinical-safety baselines exist; CI evidence gates run; and there is no orphan feature that can bypass authorization, provenance, testing or release governance.

ENG-096 | ACTIVE | 96. Official / primary references used in V2.1
Source
URL
Use
NOM-024-SSA3-2012 (PLATIICA)
https://platiica.economia.gob.mx/normalizacion/nom-024-ssa3-2012/
Official listing: vigente; SIRES/intercambio.
NOM-004-SSA3-2012 (PLATIICA)
https://platiica.economia.gob.mx/normalizacion/NOM-004-SSA3-2012/
Official listing for expediente clínico.
LFPDPPP (Cámara de Diputados)
https://www.diputados.gob.mx/LeyesBiblio/pdf/LFPDPPP.pdf
Current statutory text baseline.
NIST SP 800-207
https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/207/final
Zero Trust Architecture.
OWASP ASVS
https://owasp.org/projects/asvs
Stable ASVS 5.0.0 referenced by OWASP.
ISO 27799:2025
https://www.iso.org/standard/84647.html
Health information security controls based on ISO/IEC 27002:2022.
ONC SAFER Guides 2025
https://healthit.gov/clinical-quality-and-safety/safer-guides
EHR safety practices, high priority/results/follow-up/communications.
HL7 FHIR
https://hl7.org/fhir/
FHIR v5.0.0 R5 current published version at source check.

[ENG-096-R001] Standards/regulatory references are baselines for engineering. Applicability, certification and legal interpretation must be revalidated for the actual product scope, intended use and release date.
ENG-097 | ACTIVE | 97. Final instruction to the V2.1 implementation team
BUILD LAW No team receives the instruction “make this module”. It receives a traceable outcome, bounded domain contract, security/privacy/clinical-safety classification, data/API/event specification, test gates and exit criteria. A screen that looks complete but lacks closure, provenance, authorization or safety evidence is not complete. A backend that passes tests but increases physician cognitive load is not complete. Medical OS ships only when product, medicine and engineering agree that the workflow is safer, faster and more coherent than the process it replaces.

ENG-098 | ACTIVE | 98. Nanometric Expansion — frontend architecture
[ENG-098-R001] • Next.js is an application shell, not the clinical domain. React components consume typed application contracts and never query domain tables.
[ENG-098-R002] • Server-rendering is preferred for initial secure chart context where beneficial; high-interactivity encounter panels use client islands with explicit boundaries.
[ENG-098-R003] • Form state, remote server state and longitudinal clinical truth are different concepts. Draft UI state may be local, but accepted clinical mutations always round-trip through server policy/domain services.
[ENG-098-R004] • Optimistic UI is allowed only when rollback is clear and clinical risk is low. High-risk actions such as sign, medication finalization, critical acknowledgement and merge require confirmed server response.
[ENG-098-R005] • Route transitions must preserve patient/encounter identity context; route-level error boundaries cannot hide critical unsaved-state warnings.
ENG-099 | ACTIVE | 99. Input validation and schema strategy
Browser input → UI schema → request DTO schema → authorization → domain invariant validation → persistence
External integration → transport parser → profile/schema validation → mapping → domain invariant validation → persistence

[ENG-099-R001] • Zod or equivalent schema at every external boundary.
[ENG-099-R002] • Domain invariants are not duplicated as only client-side schemas.
[ENG-099-R003] • Unknown extra fields from external payloads are handled according to contract; never silently promoted into clinical truth.
[ENG-099-R004] • Validation errors have stable machine code, safe clinician-facing message and correlation ID where appropriate.
ENG-100 | ACTIVE | 100. Application Service pattern
export async function recordObservation(cmd, principal) {
  const ctx = await resolveTenantContext(principal);
  await authz.enforce(principal, ctx, 'observation.create', cmd.patientId);
  return db.transaction(async tx => {
    const encounter = await encounterRepo.forUpdate(tx, ctx, cmd.encounterId);
    domain.assertCanRecordObservation(encounter, cmd);
    const obs = domain.createObservation(cmd, provenance(principal));
    await observationRepo.insert(tx, ctx, obs);
    await outbox.append(tx, event('ObservationRecorded', obs));
    await audit.append(tx, auditEvent(principal, 'observation.create', obs.id));
    return obs;
  });
}

[ENG-100-R001] El transaction boundary abarca clinical write + outbox + audit cuando la consistencia del flujo lo requiere. External side effects ocurren después del commit por consumer/workflow.
ENG-101 | ACTIVE | 101. Concurrency and conflict resolution
[ENG-101-R001] • Use version/ETag/expectedVersion on clinician-edited aggregates.
[ENG-101-R002] • A stale edit never silently overwrites newer clinical data.
[ENG-101-R003] • Conflict UI shows “what changed since you opened this” for clinically meaningful collisions.
[ENG-101-R004] • Signed objects reject ordinary update regardless of expected version; amendments use a separate command.
[ENG-101-R005] • Merge/reconciliation workflows may require pessimistic locking/serialization due to patient-safety impact.
ENG-102 | ACTIVE | 102. Postgres Row Level Security defense-in-depth
-- conceptual defense; exact policy reviewed before production
ALTER TABLE clinical_problem ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
CREATE POLICY tenant_isolation ON clinical_problem
USING (tenant_id = current_setting('app.tenant_id')::uuid)
WITH CHECK (tenant_id = current_setting('app.tenant_id')::uuid);

[ENG-102-R001] RLS never replaces application authorization. Database session context must be set safely per transaction/connection and verified under pooling; failure-to-set must fail closed.
ENG-103 | ACTIVE | 103. Transactional Outbox schema
CREATE TABLE domain_outbox (
  id uuid PRIMARY KEY,
  tenant_id uuid NOT NULL,
  aggregate_type text NOT NULL,
  aggregate_id uuid NOT NULL,
  event_type text NOT NULL,
  payload jsonb NOT NULL,
  occurred_at timestamptz NOT NULL,
  published_at timestamptz,
  attempt_count int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0
);

[ENG-103-R001] • Payload minimizes PHI.
[ENG-103-R002] • Consumer deduplicates by event ID.
[ENG-103-R003] • Poison message goes to operator-visible dead-letter state.
[ENG-103-R004] • Outbox cleanup/retention policy preserves required auditability without uncontrolled growth.
ENG-104 | ACTIVE | 104. Private object storage implementation
[ENG-104-R001] • Database stores metadata, content hash, media type, size, object key and ownership; binary clinical files stay in private object storage.
[ENG-104-R002] • Object keys are unguessable and tenant ownership checked before issuing short-lived access.
[ENG-104-R003] • Upload first lands in quarantine prefix/bucket; only cleared content moves to trusted state.
[ENG-104-R004] • Server verifies content type by bytes/magic where feasible rather than trusting filename/MIME header.
[ENG-104-R005] • Large files use resumable/multipart approach with server-issued authorization; upload completion still triggers security pipeline.
ENG-105 | ACTIVE | 105. Caching policy
[ENG-105-R001] • No shared cache entry may omit tenant and authorization context from its key/partition strategy.
[ENG-105-R002] • Patient State cache is derivable and invalidated by domain events; database remains truth.
[ENG-105-R003] • Never cache signed URL beyond its expiry.
[ENG-105-R004] • Sensitive browser caching headers explicitly controlled.
[ENG-105-R005] • Redis or another cache is optional infrastructure, never a hidden source of clinical truth.
ENG-106 | ACTIVE | 106. Search and indexing engine
[ENG-106-R001] • Start with Postgres search/trigram capabilities where scale allows; introduce external search only when measured requirements justify it.
[ENG-106-R002] • External index is eventually consistent and never authorization authority.
[ENG-106-R003] • Indexed PHI requires encrypted transport/storage, tenant segregation and deletion/reindex workflows.
[ENG-106-R004] • Global search returns minimal identifying data necessary for safe selection; patient chart search is separately authorized.
ENG-107 | ACTIVE | 107. Webhooks and notification adapters
receive webhook → verify signature/timestamp → parse schema → idempotency check
→ persist inbound message → map to internal command/event → processing workflow → reconciliation

[ENG-107-R001] • Outbound webhook subscriptions are scoped and secret-rotatable.
[ENG-107-R002] • Webhook retry uses backoff and endpoint health; no infinite synchronous request chain.
[ENG-107-R003] • Notification delivery status is operational data; clinical communication acknowledgement is a distinct semantic event.
ENG-108 | ACTIVE | 108. FHIR implementation boundary
[ENG-108-R001] • FHIR R5 v5.0.0 is the current published HL7 version at V2.1 baseline, but target integration profiles may require R4/R4B/R5; adapters negotiate version explicitly.
[ENG-108-R002] • Maintain internal-to-FHIR mapping tests per resource/profile.
[ENG-108-R003] • CapabilityStatement and supported profiles/version are deployment artifacts, not marketing claims.
[ENG-108-R004] • FHIR resource IDs do not automatically become internal aggregate IDs.
[ENG-108-R005] • Provenance, Consent, AuditEvent, Patient, Encounter, Observation, Condition, AllergyIntolerance, MedicationRequest, ServiceRequest, DiagnosticReport and DocumentReference mappings are prioritized for relevant scope.
ENG-109 | ACTIVE | 109. Terminology services: SNOMED CT, LOINC and local catalogs
[ENG-109-R001] • Use terminology standards where licensed/applicable, but do not hardcode proprietary/regionally restricted distributions into source.
[ENG-109-R002] • LOINC-ready mapping for laboratory observations and reports where integration ecosystem supports it.
[ENG-109-R003] • SNOMED CT-ready concept model for clinical terminology where licensing/jurisdiction permits.

[ENG-109-R005] • Terminology server/value-set expansion is versioned; original source display/code remains attached.
ENG-110 | ACTIVE | 110. DICOM / DICOMweb engineering details

ENG-111 | ACTIVE | 111. OpenTelemetry and security telemetry

ENG-114 | ACTIVE | 114. ISO 27001 / SOC 2 readiness without false claims
[ENG-114-R001] V2.1 may structure controls/evidence so future ISO/IEC 27001 certification or SOC 2 examination is easier, but the application must never state it is “ISO certified” or “SOC 2 compliant” without the appropriate organizational audit/certification scope and evidence. ISO 27799:2025 is used as health-specific security guidance layered onto the information-security program.
ENG-115 | ACTIVE | 115. Regulatory CDS / ScDM branch
[ENG-115-R001] Each high-impact intelligence feature enters the Regulatory Feature Classification Register before implementation. FDA’s January 2026 final Clinical Decision Support Software guidance is a reference for the U.S. market; Mexico assessment uses current medical-device framework and intended use. V2.1 explicitly avoids assuming that all clinical decision support is either regulated or exempt.
[ENG-115-R002] • Document intended user/purpose, input, output, autonomy and time criticality.
[ENG-115-R003] • Document whether physician can independently review the basis of recommendation.
[ENG-115-R004] • If classified as device/ScDM path, activate quality/regulatory design controls, validation and change-control obligations appropriate to jurisdiction.
[ENG-115-R005] • Patient-facing diagnostic/therapeutic functions receive separate assessment.

Required exercise

Object storage access failure
metadata/chart degraded behavior
Workflow runtime outage
resume timers/events without duplication
Credential compromise
rotate/revoke and verify
AI provider outage
core clinical system continues; fallback/disable

ENG-126 | ACTIVE | 126. API authentication, CORS and service identities

ENG-127 | ACTIVE | 127. Abuse prevention and rate limiting
[ENG-127-R001] • Per-actor/tenant/IP-class limits for login, OTP, global search, patient-share verification and AI-heavy endpoints.
[ENG-127-R002] • Limits must not block legitimate emergency workflow without documented fallback/escalation.
[ENG-127-R003] • Brute-force/share-token abuse generates security signal.
[ENG-127-R004] • Expensive exports/analytics use queued jobs with authorization recheck at execution time.
ENG-128 | ACTIVE | 128. UX coding laws
[ENG-128-R001] • One primary action per panel where possible.
[ENG-128-R002] • No chained modal workflow for encounter.
[ENG-128-R003] • Every async mutation exposes saving/saved/error/conflict state.
[ENG-128-R004] • Destructive/high-risk actions have contextual confirmation, not generic “Are you sure?”.
[ENG-128-R005] • Patient identity remains visually persistent during clinical action.
[ENG-128-R006] • AI proposal uses diff/accept/edit/reject rather than silently filling signed fields.

ENG-130 | ACTIVE | 130. Abrupt final improvement — no-orphan engineering law
NANOMETRIC FREEZE Después de la auditoría final, V2.1 adopta una última regla superior: ningún requisito, dato, riesgo, alerta, obligación, modelo, integración, norma o pantalla puede existir sin owner, lifecycle, provenance/evidence y mecanismo de verificación proporcional a su riesgo. Si no puede demostrarse cómo se autoriza, prueba, observa, recupera y retira, todavía no está diseñado.
