ENG-132 | ACTIVE | 132. Final V2.1 declaration after massive + nanometric improvement
ENGINEERING BASELINE READY V2.1 no debe crecer por acumulación de features. A partir de este punto, el trabajo correcto es implementar por niveles y sólo abrir una modificación del baseline cuando aparezca evidencia clínica, técnica, regulatoria, de seguridad o de usabilidad que demuestre que el diseño debe cambiar. V2 define el Medical OS que queremos; V2.1 define el sistema de ingeniería que impide construirlo de forma mediocre.


ENG-133 | ACTIVE | 133. V2.1.1 hyperscale engineering amendment — purpose and authority
[ENG-133-R001] This amendment promotes the architecture discoveries from the Agent Execution Companion into the authoritative engineering specification. V2 remains the frozen product/clinical WHAT. V2.1.1 remains the engineering HOW. The Agent Execution Companion remains the operational execution layer for humans and coding agents.
[ENG-133-R002] This amendment does not claim that Medical OS is perfect, clinically validated, certified, compliant, or safe for unrestricted production. It defines additional engineering obligations required before those claims could be evaluated.
[ENG-133-R003] Source hierarchy: V2 product/clinical specification → V2.1.1 engineering specification → scoped agent execution instructions → code/tests/evidence.
[ENG-133-R004] No operational instruction may weaken a V2/V2.1.1 clinical, security, privacy, reliability or regulatory requirement.
[ENG-133-R005] When implementation discovers a product-level contradiction, stop and classify it rather than silently changing product behavior.
ENG-134 | ACTIVE | 134. 150-expert equivalent engineering organization
[ENG-134-R001] Medical OS shall be engineered as if reviewed by a 150-expert multidisciplinary organization, while keeping one accountable DRI per runtime behavior.
[ENG-134-R002] Executive Architecture & Systems Theory: 10.
[ENG-134-R003] Clinical Workflow & Physician Experience: 14.
[ENG-134-R004] Clinical Domain / Longitudinal State: 14.
[ENG-134-R005] Medication / Orders / Results / Follow-up: 12.
[ENG-134-R006] Knowledge Engineering / Terminology: 14.
[ENG-134-R007] Deterministic Algorithms / Numerical Safety: 10.
[ENG-134-R008] AI / Retrieval / Document Intelligence: 14.
[ENG-134-R009] Data Platform / Search / Eventing: 12.
[ENG-134-R010] Security / Privacy / Identity / Cryptography: 12.
[ENG-134-R011] Frontend / Design System / Human Factors: 12.
[ENG-134-R012] Interoperability / Country Packs / Integrations: 8.
[ENG-134-R013] SRE / Performance / DR / Chaos: 8.
[ENG-134-R014] QA / Verification / Release Evidence: 10.
[ENG-134-R015] Independent physician, pharmacy, laboratory, radiology, nursing, regulatory, privacy and penetration-test reviewers remain separate assurance functions and are not replaced by engineering.
ENG-135 | ACTIVE | 135. Clinical semantic kernel
[ENG-135-R001] Create a small dependency-light kernel for PatientRef, EncounterRef, TenantRef, ClinicalQuantity, ClinicalCode, ClinicalDate, ClinicalInstant, SourceRef, ProvenanceRef, AlgorithmVersionRef, KnowledgeArtifactRef, InformationState and VerificationStatus.
[ENG-135-R002] Use opaque/branded identifiers so PatientId, EncounterId, TenantId and OrganizationId cannot be interchanged accidentally.
[ENG-135-R003] The kernel cannot depend on AI providers, UI frameworks, specialty packs or external integration adapters.
ENG-136 | ACTIVE | 136. Clinical quantity, unit algebra and exact decimal policy
[ENG-136-R001] C4/C5 arithmetic uses an approved exact-decimal strategy with explicit scale and rounding mode.
[ENG-136-R002] Clinical quantities preserve original value/unit, normalized value/unit, dimension, precision, comparator and conversion provenance.
[ENG-136-R003] Dimensionally incompatible quantities fail validation; no implicit conversion by string matching.
[ENG-136-R004] Dose semantics distinguish amount, rate, daily dose, frequency, duration, route, concentration and measurable administration amount.
[ENG-136-R005] Binary floating point is not authoritative for safety-critical dose/threshold logic unless an ADR and validation prove it appropriate.
ENG-137 | ACTIVE | 137. Computation authority constitution
[ENG-137-R001] Every capability declares its computation authority and mutation authority.
[ENG-137-R002] Authorities: DETERMINISTIC, KNOWLEDGE, AI_ASSISTED, PHYSICIAN or explicitly governed HYBRID.
[ENG-137-R003] Mutation authority: READ_ONLY, DRAFT_ONLY, PHYSICIAN_COMMIT or SYSTEM_COMMIT.
[ENG-137-R004] Exact arithmetic, authorization, validated formulas, unit conversion and state transitions cannot delegate authority to generative AI.
[ENG-137-R005] AI-degraded mode must preserve the deterministic clinical core.
ENG-138 | ACTIVE | 138. Algorithm Platform — separation of planes
[ENG-138-R001] The algorithm subsystem is divided into AUTHORING, VALIDATION, REGISTRY/RELEASE and RUNTIME planes. No generated algorithm can move directly from authoring to production.
[ENG-138-R002] Authoring creates typed contract, formula/logic, applicability, sources, units, rounding and failure semantics.
[ENG-138-R003] Validation owns independent oracles, golden cases, property, metamorphic, mutation, fuzz and boundary testing.
[ENG-138-R004] Registry owns immutable manifests, signatures/hashes, lifecycle, activation and retirement.
[ENG-138-R005] Runtime executes only approved compatible versions and emits lineage/explanation.
ENG-139 | ACTIVE | 139. Algorithm manifest and reproducibility
[ENG-139-R001] Manifest records algorithm ID/version/hash, intended purpose, source versions, schemas, unit policy, rounding, applicability, exclusions, missing-data behavior, golden corpus, reviewers, status, activation and retirement.
[ENG-139-R002] Every displayed high-impact calculated result can identify the algorithm/version used.
[ENG-139-R003] Historical versions remain replayable/buildable or have preserved executable artifact plus sufficient manifest/source for investigation.
ENG-140 | ACTIVE | 140. Algorithm Factory
[ENG-140-R001] Provide a standardized generator that accelerates algorithm creation without auto-approving clinical content.
[ENG-140-R002] Generate typed TypeScript contract/function skeleton, Zod input/output schemas, unit contracts, manifest, test skeleton, property-test skeleton, golden fixtures, benchmark harness and registry entry.
[ENG-140-R003] Pipeline: requirement → source → contract → applicability → units/rounding → implementation → independent reference → generated tests → clinical review → manifest → shadow → release.
[ENG-140-R004] Clinical thresholds, contraindications and dosing rules must come from approved sources and clinical governance, never coding-agent invention.
ENG-141 | ACTIVE | 141. Algorithm verification strategy
[ENG-141-R001] C5 algorithms require 100% pass of declared safety-critical invariants and approved reference/golden cases before release.
[ENG-141-R002] Use equivalence classes and exact boundaries instead of pretending to enumerate astronomical state spaces.
[ENG-141-R003] Use independent reference implementation where feasible and avoid shared helper logic that can duplicate the same defect.
[ENG-141-R004] Mutation testing targets operators, thresholds, units, rounding, negation and branch order.
[ENG-141-R005] Shadow execution compares candidate and active versions before cutover.
ENG-142 | ACTIVE | 142. Typed algorithm outcomes
[ENG-142-R001] Algorithm failure is never represented as a plausible clinical value.
[ENG-142-R002] Required outcomes include SUCCESS, NOT_APPLICABLE, INSUFFICIENT_INFORMATION, INVALID_INPUT, UNSUPPORTED, DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE and INTERNAL_ERROR.
[ENG-142-R003] 0, null, empty string and NaN cannot ambiguously mean calculation failure.
[ENG-142-R004] UI must preserve the distinction through presentation.
ENG-143 | ACTIVE | 143. Derived-fact lineage and invalidation
[ENG-143-R001] Derived facts trace to exact source facts, verification state, units, algorithm/version, knowledge/version when used and execution time.
[ENG-143-R002] Dependency changes mark affected derived facts stale and trigger governed recomputation.
[ENG-143-R003] Incremental computation and full deterministic rebuild must be cross-checkable.
ENG-144 | ACTIVE | 144. Knowledge Engine — architectural planes
[ENG-144-R001] Knowledge Engine is a versioned computable clinical knowledge platform, not an LLM prompt library.
[ENG-144-R002] SOURCE → EVIDENCE → SEMANTIC → COMPILATION → VALIDATION → RELEASE → RUNTIME → OBSERVABILITY.
[ENG-144-R003] There is no Source→Runtime shortcut.
[ENG-144-R004] The LLM is not the knowledge layer of record.
ENG-145 | ACTIVE | 145. Knowledge Source Registry
[ENG-145-R001] Track issuer, title, jurisdiction, specialty, publication/effective/retrieval dates, supersession, source identifier, content hash, license, authority tier, evidence type, status and freshness owner.
[ENG-145-R002] Recency and authority are separate dimensions; newest is not automatically strongest.
[ENG-145-R003] A detected source change opens impact review and never silently changes production behavior.
ENG-146 | ACTIVE | 146. Evidence propositions and typed Knowledge IR
[ENG-146-R001] Normalize guidance into atomic propositions: population, condition, action, strength, exceptions, timing, threshold/unit, evidence, jurisdiction and effective period.
[ENG-146-R002] Compile propositions to a restricted typed intermediate representation.
[ENG-146-R003] IR supports explicit temporal operators, comparisons, bounded quantifiers, exceptions and deterministic explanation trace.
[ENG-146-R004] UNKNOWN and CONFLICT cannot silently collapse to FALSE; NOT_APPLICABLE remains distinct.
ENG-147 | ACTIVE | 147. Knowledge static analysis
[ENG-147-R001] Compiler detects unreachable rules, contradictory branches, missing units, undefined terminology, circular dependencies, ambiguous exceptions, uncovered unknown states, impossible intervals and unresolved priority ties.
[ENG-147-R002] Clinical rules cannot fire solely on keyword presence.
[ENG-147-R003] Negation, exception precedence and temporal semantics receive dedicated mutation/adversarial tests.
ENG-148 | ACTIVE | 148. Knowledge applicability and jurisdiction
[ENG-148-R001] Applicability may depend on age, life stage, pregnancy/context, jurisdiction, problem state, medication, renal/hepatic state, encounter context and time.
[ENG-148-R002] Missing applicability facts produce unknown/insufficient-information state rather than defaults.
[ENG-148-R003] Resolve global guidance, law/regulation, national guideline, institution policy and specialty packs through a governed jurisdiction/precedence lattice.
[ENG-148-R004] Unresolved clinically meaningful conflict is surfaced rather than hidden.
ENG-149 | ACTIVE | 149. Knowledge release and behavioral diff
[ENG-149-R001] Lifecycle: DRAFT → TECHNICALLY_VALIDATED → CLINICALLY_REVIEWED → SHADOW → APPROVED → CANARY → ACTIVE → SUPERSEDED/RETIRED.
[ENG-149-R002] Before activation compare old/new behavior over synthetic cohorts: newly triggered, removed, severity-changed, timing-changed and population-changed outputs.
[ENG-149-R003] Estimate blast radius for high-impact changes before activation using privacy-governed aggregate analysis.
[ENG-149-R004] Approved packages are content-addressed and integrity-verified before runtime loading.
ENG-150 | ACTIVE | 150. Knowledge freshness and incident impact
[ENG-150-R001] High-risk artifacts have explicit review intervals and freshness SLOs.
[ENG-150-R002] Expired knowledge enters visible governance/degraded state according to policy; it is not silently assumed current.
[ENG-150-R003] Given an artifact/version, incident tooling can identify executions and potentially affected downstream recommendations/actions.
ENG-151 | ACTIVE | 151. Terminology infrastructure
[ENG-151-R001] Terminology is versioned infrastructure with code systems, value sets, concept maps, synonyms, retirement and provenance.
[ENG-151-R002] Preserve original external code/text alongside normalized concepts.
[ENG-151-R003] Terminology version is part of the clinical version vector for high-impact interpretation.
ENG-152 | ACTIVE | 152. Sparse temporal Patient Clinical Graph
[ENG-152-R001] Patient graph contains clinically meaningful nodes and typed temporal edges only; never materialize every possible relationship.
[ENG-152-R002] Patient graph and Knowledge graph remain logically separate.
[ENG-152-R003] Graph queries are bounded by purpose, edge type, depth and time window.
[ENG-152-R004] Every graph-derived clinical fact remains source/provenance linked.
ENG-153 | ACTIVE | 153. Dependency DAG and incremental computation
[ENG-153-R001] Derived facts and knowledge evaluations declare exact dependencies.
[ENG-153-R002] A changed fact invalidates only dependent computations whenever possible.
[ENG-153-R003] Cycles are rejected unless an explicit fixed-point semantic is defined and tested.
[ENG-153-R004] Full rebuild remains available as reconciliation oracle against incremental state.
ENG-154 | ACTIVE | 154. Hyperscale state-space strategy
[ENG-154-R001] Medical OS does not enumerate all theoretical combinations of millions/billions/trillions of variables. It controls combinatorial complexity.
[ENG-154-R002] Use bounded domains, sparse state, dependency-local recomputation, equivalence classes, property testing, covering arrays and risk-targeted higher-order combinations.
[ENG-154-R003] Use model checking/formal methods selectively for catastrophic invariants where tractable.
[ENG-154-R004] Capacity planning sizes actual cardinalities, event rates, payload bytes, graph edges, indexes and query patterns—not theoretical variable combinations.
ENG-155 | ACTIVE | 155. Consistency classes
[ENG-155-R001] STRONG: identity, signed chart, medication orders, critical-result acknowledgement, privilege changes.
[ENG-155-R002] BOUNDED_STALE: Patient State projections and search indexes.
[ENG-155-R003] EVENTUAL: analytics and noncritical counters.
[ENG-155-R004] UI/read models must know and expose clinically relevant freshness/degradation.
ENG-156 | ACTIVE | 156. Temporal and bitemporal semantics
[ENG-156-R001] Model effective clinical time, event time, recorded time, received time and corrected time separately where relevant.
[ENG-156-R002] For selected facts preserve valid-time and system-time so the platform can answer what was clinically true and what Medical OS knew at a historical moment.
[ENG-156-R003] Clinical age/window/date calculations use explicit timezone/jurisdiction semantics.
ENG-157 | ACTIVE | 157. Data contract registry and schema evolution
[ENG-157-R001] Every API/event/schema has owner, version, compatibility policy, PHI classification, retention, consumers, SLIs and deprecation plan.
[ENG-157-R002] Clinical semantic changes require semantic-diff review, not merely syntactic schema compatibility.
[ENG-157-R003] Removing a field cannot erase historical clinical meaning.
ENG-158 | ACTIVE | 158. Data quality propagation
[ENG-158-R001] Track completeness, validity, plausibility, consistency, timeliness, provenance, verification, uniqueness and terminology resolution per fact/purpose.
[ENG-158-R002] Derived facts inherit relevant uncertainty/verification constraints.
[ENG-158-R003] AI-extracted unverified values cannot silently become verified safety-critical inputs.
ENG-159 | ACTIVE | 159. Patient identity and merge safety at scale
[ENG-159-R001] MPI uses normalized identifiers, deterministic matching and governed probabilistic candidate generation only where justified.
[ENG-159-R002] Merge requires preview, collision detection, provenance, result/obligation reconciliation and reversible lineage.
[ENG-159-R003] Audit history is never rewritten to pretend two identities were always one.
[ENG-159-R004] Cross-tenant merge is prohibited by default.
ENG-160 | ACTIVE | 160. Search and wrong-patient defense
[ENG-160-R001] Search index is not an authorization authority or clinical source of truth.
[ENG-160-R002] Search results emphasize disambiguating identity attributes.
[ENG-160-R003] High-impact actions rebind/validate patient context server-side.
[ENG-160-R004] Recent-patient ranking cannot override exact identifiers.
ENG-161 | ACTIVE | 161. Patient State projection contract
[ENG-161-R001] Pipeline: authoritative facts → normalized facts → lifecycle state → deterministic derived facts → knowledge evaluations → obligations → longitudinal summaries → optional AI assistive views.
[ENG-161-R002] Snapshots include as-of version/time, source watermark, algorithm/knowledge versions and degraded/completeness flags.
[ENG-161-R003] Projection versions can be shadow-compared before cutover.
ENG-162 | ACTIVE | 162. Result, correction and longitudinal baseline semantics
[ENG-162-R001] Received ≠ reviewed ≠ actioned ≠ patient informed ≠ closed.
[ENG-162-R002] Corrected/amended result links to prior versions, invalidates dependent derived facts and can reopen review/obligation workflows.
[ENG-162-R003] Personal historical baseline, population/reference interval and episode baseline are distinct concepts and labeled separately.
[ENG-162-R004] Trend comparability requires compatible analyte, unit, method/context and effective time.
ENG-163 | ACTIVE | 163. Clinical Obligation Graph and no-orphan reconciliation
[ENG-163-R001] Obligations may originate from plan, order, result, medication monitoring, referral, preventive care or knowledge artifact.
[ENG-163-R002] Every obligation has owner, due state, source, escalation, completion evidence and lifecycle.
[ENG-163-R003] Independent reconciler detects results without review/owner, plans without obligations, monitoring without follow-up, failed communications, stuck workflows and stale projections.
[ENG-163-R004] Snooze never equals closure.
ENG-164 | ACTIVE | 164. Durable workflow and external side-effect ledger
[ENG-164-R001] Long-running clinical workflows use durable state, idempotent activities, explicit retries and reconciliation.
[ENG-164-R002] External side effects distinguish intended, attempted, provider-accepted, provider-confirmed, failed, ambiguous and reconciled.
[ENG-164-R003] Timeout is not equivalent to failure.
[ENG-164-R004] Idempotency exists at request, domain command, outbox, provider and reconciliation layers where applicable.
ENG-165 | ACTIVE | 165. Concurrency and signed-record immutability
[ENG-165-R001] Mutable clinical aggregates use optimistic concurrency/version checks plus database constraints for invariants.
[ENG-165-R002] Conflict UX preserves both clinicians’ work.
[ENG-165-R003] Signing creates immutable semantic snapshot with provenance.
[ENG-165-R004] Corrections are append-only amendments; signed content is never silently overwritten.
ENG-166 | ACTIVE | 166. Document Intelligence production pipeline
[ENG-166-R001] Upload → quarantine/malware → immutable original → render/OCR/layout → candidate extraction → semantic mapping → provenance/confidence → human verification → structured fact.
[ENG-166-R002] Candidate patient identifiers are compared to selected patient; mismatch/ambiguity blocks automatic association.
[ENG-166-R003] Correction preserves original extraction and reviewer lineage and invalidates downstream derived facts.
[ENG-166-R004] OCR/AI extraction is not verified clinical truth.
ENG-167 | ACTIVE | 167. AI Task Fabric and Gateway
[ENG-167-R001] Each AI use case has task ID/version, intended/prohibited use, risk tier, context contract, allowed data, knowledge policy, tools, schema, timeout, evals and human authority.
[ENG-167-R002] Domain code cannot import provider model SDKs directly.
[ENG-167-R003] Gateway performs task routing, minimum-necessary context compilation, provider/model policy, schema validation, telemetry and kill switches.
[ENG-167-R004] AI tasks cannot silently inherit permissions from prompt text.
ENG-168 | ACTIVE | 168. AI evidence, context and prompt-injection controls
[ENG-168-R001] Patient data and approved knowledge retrieval are separate authorized namespaces.
[ENG-168-R002] Retrieved document/message/web text is treated as data, never system instruction.
[ENG-168-R003] Async AI responses are patient/tenant/encounter/request bound and discarded when stale.
[ENG-168-R004] Context truncation is observable and can force abstention.
[ENG-168-R005] High-impact evidence-grounded claims link to supplied source IDs/spans where feasible.
ENG-169 | ACTIVE | 169. AI authority and evaluation
[ENG-169-R001] C4/C5 AI cannot sign, prescribe, close critical results, confirm diagnoses, alter verified facts or complete high-impact obligations without explicit authorized human action under the current intended use.
[ENG-169-R002] Per-task evals include factual support, omission, harmful fabrication, citation accuracy, schema validity, abstention, latency, robustness, prompt injection, wrong-patient context and human-factors anchoring.
[ENG-169-R003] Aggregate improvement cannot hide regression in a critical safety slice.
[ENG-169-R004] AI-disabled mode preserves the clinical core.
ENG-170 | ACTIVE | 170. Interface structural shell and information hierarchy
[ENG-170-R001] Desktop baseline: persistent patient identity/state rail, central Clinical Workspace, contextual Intelligence Rail.
[ENG-170-R002] Priority: patient identity → immediate safety → current task → current state → pending obligations/results → supporting history → AI/secondary intelligence.
[ENG-170-R003] AI cannot dominate or obscure the physician’s primary task.
ENG-171 | ACTIVE | 171. Clinical information-state language
[ENG-171-R001] Distinguish UNKNOWN, NOT_ASSESSED, ABSENT, NEGATIVE, PENDING, UNAVAILABLE, NOT_APPLICABLE, UNVERIFIED, VERIFIED and AI_SUGGESTED.
[ENG-171-R002] Loading, empty-confirmed, filtered-empty, stale, degraded, permission-denied, unavailable, conflict and error are separate UI microstates.
[ENG-171-R003] One dash, blank cell or green check cannot collapse semantically different states.
ENG-172 | ACTIVE | 172. Nanostructural UI contracts
[ENG-172-R001] Every high-risk surface documents user, patient/encounter context, read model, freshness, mutation, permission, information states, loading/error/degraded behavior and tests.
[ENG-172-R002] Every visible clinical status/control maps to a domain state/source/action/permission/test.
[ENG-172-R003] No decorative clinical-looking indicator without semantic contract.
ENG-173 | ACTIVE | 173. Button, link and form-field contracts
[ENG-173-R001] Button: semantic action, preconditions, permission, target, side effect, idempotency, loading, success evidence, failure/recovery, audit, keyboard/touch.
[ENG-173-R002] Link: context transition, patient/encounter binding, external marking, referrer/query PHI policy and safe deep-link failure.
[ENG-173-R003] Field: semantic type, source, requiredness rationale, unknown behavior, units, validation, precision, default policy, provenance, edit permissions and downstream dependencies.
[ENG-173-R004] No clinical default to normal/negative/zero/today/current weight merely to save clicks.
ENG-174 | ACTIVE | 174. Patient-switch global invariant
[ENG-174-R001] All patient-bound UI stores, queries, subscriptions, jobs, AI tasks, modals, drafts, caches and uploads carry tenant/patient identity.
[ENG-174-R002] Switching patient cancels, invalidates or quarantines stale work before the new workspace becomes actionable.
[ENG-174-R003] Old-patient skeleton/data cannot remain visible beneath a new-patient header.
[ENG-174-R004] Wrong-patient chaos test is a release gate for patient-bound architecture changes.
ENG-175 | ACTIVE | 175. Human factors and interruption recovery
[ENG-175-R001] Maintain hazard log for wrong patient, wrong dose, hidden result, ambiguous unit, alert overload, destructive defaults, interruption and lost drafts.
[ENG-175-R002] After interruption the physician can immediately identify patient, current task, unsaved state, new information and pending decision.
[ENG-175-R003] Session reauthentication preserves drafts but revalidates authorization.
[ENG-175-R004] High-volume encounter paths support keyboard-first operation without bypassing safety.
ENG-176 | ACTIVE | 176. 2,000-physician synthetic validation program
[ENG-176-R001] A synthetic cohort of 2,000 physician personas is an adversarial design/testing instrument, not evidence that 2,000 real physicians were consulted.
[ENG-176-R002] Stratify by specialty, experience, setting, volume, digital fluency, device, interruption rate and documentation style.
[ENG-176-R003] Measure task success, time to decision, clicks, repeated entry, uncertainty points, unsafe interpretations, ignored alerts, information not found, workarounds and abandonment.
[ENG-176-R004] Do not average away severe minority-cohort safety failures.
[ENG-176-R005] C4/C5 synthetic success advances a design to real-clinician validation; it does not approve production.
ENG-177 | ACTIVE | 177. Digital Twin Clinic
[ENG-177-R001] Maintain an accelerated synthetic clinic spanning months/years of virtual care.
[ENG-177-R002] Simulate visits, chronic disease, polypharmacy, labs, imaging, corrected results, referrals, messages, no-shows, clinician turnover, knowledge updates, migrations and outages.
[ENG-177-R003] Simulate physician, nursing, administrative, laboratory and patient actions with independent clocks/failures.
[ENG-177-R004] Synthetic distributions are documented test constructs, not epidemiologic truth.
ENG-178 | ACTIVE | 178. 30-pass adversarial assurance circuit

ENG-179 | ACTIVE | 179. 30-pass defect ledger and restart rule
[ENG-179-R001] Each defect records pass, capability, scenario, severity, likelihood, detectability, patient/security impact, root cause, violated invariant, prevention, detection, recovery, owner, tests and evidence.
[ENG-179-R002] If the same root-cause class recurs, redesign the shared primitive rather than patching locally.
[ENG-179-R003] A major architecture correction invalidates affected later passes and those passes rerun.
[ENG-179-R004] No unresolved S0/S1 enters controlled pilot.
ENG-180 | ACTIVE | 180. Global invariant catalog
[ENG-180-R001] Tenant isolation.
[ENG-180-R002] Patient-context binding.
[ENG-180-R003] Signed-record immutability.
[ENG-180-R004] Provenance preservation.
[ENG-180-R005] No silent UNKNOWN→NEGATIVE.
[ENG-180-R006] No silent AI authority escalation.
[ENG-180-R007] No orphan critical work.
[ENG-180-R008] Deterministic safety core.
[ENG-180-R009] Idempotent/reconciled external effects.
[ENG-180-R010] Reconstructable high-impact decisions.
ENG-181 | ACTIVE | 181. Property registry and formal-method candidates
[ENG-181-R001] Maintain machine-readable global properties linking prevention, tests, reconciliation and monitoring.
[ENG-181-R002] Use model checking/formal specification selectively for tenant isolation, patient binding, signed immutability, result/obligation lifecycles, selected distributed retry semantics and dose/unit invariants where tractable.
[ENG-181-R003] Formal methods supplement, not replace, clinical validation and production monitoring.
ENG-182 | ACTIVE | 182. Security zero-trust request path
[ENG-182-R001] Every high-impact request follows: authenticate principal → resolve trusted tenant → authorize resource/action → validate patient/encounter context → validate schema → enforce domain invariant → transact → audit/outbox → minimum-necessary response.
[ENG-182-R002] tenant_id/shard key from client is never authoritative.
[ENG-182-R003] CORS is not authorization.
[ENG-182-R004] Search, cache, vector store and object storage each re-enforce authorization boundaries.
ENG-183 | ACTIVE | 183. Authorization policy as code and break-glass
[ENG-183-R001] Policies are versioned/testable and consider tenant, role, relationship, purpose, resource state and break-glass context where applicable.
[ENG-183-R002] Break-glass is explicit, reasoned, time-bound, highly audited and reviewed.
[ENG-183-R003] There is no hidden universal support/admin bypass.
ENG-184 | ACTIVE | 184. Cryptographic architecture and key lifecycle
[ENG-184-R001] Define encryption in transit, at rest and application/envelope encryption where justified.
[ENG-184-R002] Define KMS/HSM boundary, key hierarchy, data-encryption keys, key-encryption keys, rotation, revocation, backup-key recovery and separation of duties.
[ENG-184-R003] Prefer short-lived workload identity over static credentials.
[ENG-184-R004] Key rotation and recovery are drilled, not merely documented.
[ENG-184-R005] No home-grown cryptographic primitives.
ENG-185 | ACTIVE | 185. PHI egress and data minimization
[ENG-185-R001] Catalog every PHI egress route: AI, communications, interoperability, exports, support and analytics.
[ENG-185-R002] Allowed payload is compiled from task/purpose policy rather than serializing entire patient object then deleting fields.
[ENG-185-R003] Structured allowlist logging excludes notes, documents, prompts and raw request bodies by default.
[ENG-185-R004] Sentinel tests detect PHI/secrets leakage into logs/provider payloads.
ENG-186 | ACTIVE | 186. Audit ledger and completeness reconciliation
[ENG-186-R001] Audit captures actor, action, resource, outcome, reason/context and version without unnecessary PHI.
[ENG-186-R002] Audit storage is append-oriented, access-controlled and integrity-monitored.
[ENG-186-R003] Critical domain writes are reconciled against expected audit events; missing audit is a production defect.
ENG-187 | ACTIVE | 187. Storage, object integrity and reconciliation
[ENG-187-R001] Objects are private, content-hashed, metadata-bound, authorization-checked and lifecycle-governed.
[ENG-187-R002] Original clinical documents remain immutable.
[ENG-187-R003] Detect DB rows with missing objects, objects without valid metadata, incomplete uploads and retention mismatches.
ENG-188 | ACTIVE | 188. Hyperscale database and partitioning strategy
[ENG-188-R001] Avoid premature physical sharding; enforce logical shard/tenant/patient routing contracts first.
[ENG-188-R002] Candidate partition axes include tenant, patient, time, event class and artifact type based on measured workload.
[ENG-188-R003] High-frequency patient-local reads/writes should remain colocated where practical.
[ENG-188-R004] Population analytics uses separate governed analytical paths rather than unbounded transactional cross-patient joins.
ENG-189 | ACTIVE | 189. Multi-region and consistency
[ENG-189-R001] Define home region, residency, write topology, replication lag, failover and clock assumptions.
[ENG-189-R002] Do not promise active-active clinical writes until conflict semantics are explicitly specified and tested.
[ENG-189-R003] Global opaque IDs tolerate multi-region generation without coordination bottlenecks.
ENG-190 | ACTIVE | 190. Query, graph and cache budgets
[ENG-190-R001] Interactive endpoints define max rows, pagination, fan-out and latency budgets.
[ENG-190-R002] Unbounded ORM relations and graph traversals are prohibited in high-cardinality paths.
[ENG-190-R003] Cache entries define authority, key scope, TTL, invalidation, stale policy and PHI classification.
[ENG-190-R004] Safety-critical writes revalidate authoritative state rather than trusting cache.
ENG-191 | ACTIVE | 191. Performance and capacity engineering
[ENG-191-R001] Model patients, encounters/day, observations/encounter, documents, bytes, jobs, messages, search QPS, AI calls and growth using distributions and pathological tenants.
[ENG-191-R002] Maintain long-chart benchmarks, skewed multi-tenant load, reconnect storms and hours-long browser soak tests.
[ENG-191-R003] Targets use measured p50/p95/p99 by critical journey; do not invent decorative availability/latency numbers.
ENG-192 | ACTIVE | 192. SRE clinical service catalog and SLOs
[ENG-192-R001] Every critical service/domain has owner, tier, dependencies, SLOs, dashboards, alerts, runbook, capacity model and DR class.
[ENG-192-R002] Clinical SLIs include critical-result visibility latency, obligation scheduler lag, Patient State freshness, signed-record durability, audit success and algorithm/knowledge resolution.
[ENG-192-R003] Wrong-patient mutation, cross-tenant disclosure, signed-record corruption and silent critical-result loss are safety incidents, not normal error-budget consumption.
ENG-193 | ACTIVE | 193. Chaos engineering and fault injection
[ENG-193-R001] Inject latency, timeout, duplicate, reordering, malformed payload, permission revocation, stale cache, DB/provider outage, artifact corruption and uncertain commit.
[ENG-193-R002] Chaos passes only if clinical invariants and reconciliation remain correct.
[ENG-193-R003] Queue/database/provider chaos is exercised independently and in selected combinations.
ENG-194 | ACTIVE | 194. Disaster recovery and safe mode
[ENG-194-R001] Define and measure RPO/RTO separately for chart, documents, audit, artifacts and workflows.
[ENG-194-R002] Restore drills verify signed records, documents, audit, algorithms/knowledge and workflow state.
[ENG-194-R003] External side effects are reconciled after restore to avoid blind resend.
[ENG-194-R004] Safe mode preserves patient lookup, chart read, critical work visibility, basic documentation, deterministic core and audit during selected dependency failures.
ENG-195 | ACTIVE | 195. Release rings and Clinical Bill of Materials
[ENG-195-R001] Release rings: local → CI → ephemeral → integration → synthetic shadow clinic → internal → clinician sandbox → canary tenant → controlled pilot → wider production.
[ENG-195-R002] Clinical BOM identifies app commit, DB migration, algorithm set, knowledge set, terminology, AI task/model config, country/specialty packs and relevant clinical configuration.
[ENG-195-R003] Runtime verifies compatibility/integrity before high-risk computation becomes available.
ENG-196 | ACTIVE | 196. Semantic diffs
[ENG-196-R001] Clinical semantic diff: thresholds, units, required data, workflow transitions, severity and intended clinical meaning.
[ENG-196-R002] Database semantic diff: destructive operations, nullability/default/state changes, backfill assumptions, lock/index risk and historical meaning.
[ENG-196-R003] UI semantic diff: identity visibility, critical status, authority-changing controls and defaults.
[ENG-196-R004] Security semantic diff: permissions, egress, trust boundaries, secrets, public routes and crypto usage.
[ENG-196-R005] AI semantic diff: model, prompt policy, retrieval, tools, schema, risk tier and eval slices.
[ENG-196-R006] Knowledge semantic diff: populations, exceptions, thresholds, actions, sources and effective dates.
ENG-197 | ACTIVE | 197. Change Impact Engine
[ENG-197-R001] Given changed code/schema/algorithm/knowledge/terminology/prompt, identify dependent capabilities, owners, tests, workflows and required review councils.
[ENG-197-R002] High-risk incidents/releases support Patient Impact Query by version/artifact/model/task.
[ENG-197-R003] Data Lineage Query traces a displayed fact backward to source and forward to dependent calculations/alerts/obligations/actions.
ENG-198 | ACTIVE | 198. Clinical Action Receipt and version vector
[ENG-198-R001] Selected high-impact actions generate an internal receipt: actor, patient, encounter, intent, source facts, safety checks, versions, outcome, side effects and audit references.
[ENG-198-R002] Version vector includes app, schema, algorithm, knowledge, terminology, AI task/model config, country/specialty pack and policy where relevant.
[ENG-198-R003] Deterministic computation/knowledge can be historically replayed from preserved source facts and version vector.
ENG-199 | ACTIVE | 199. Testing architecture
[ENG-199-R001] Risk-driven layers: type/static → unit → property → integration → contract → E2E → adversarial → performance → chaos → clinical validation.
[ENG-199-R002] Expected values are not generated by the same implementation under test.
[ENG-199-R003] Synthetic Patient Factory spans life stages, multimorbidity, polypharmacy, missing/conflicting data and boundary cases.
[ENG-199-R004] Model-based workflow testing generates transition sequences for results, obligations, prescriptions and signing.
ENG-200 | ACTIVE | 200. Combinatorial testing
[ENG-200-R001] Use pairwise/t-wise covering arrays for large configuration matrices plus risk-targeted higher-order scenarios.
[ENG-200-R002] Maintain N-squared interaction matrices across patient, medication, result, obligation, document, knowledge, AI, security and infrastructure domains.
[ENG-200-R003] Astronomical theoretical state space is never presented as exhaustively tested.
ENG-201 | ACTIVE | 201. Supply-chain and release evidence
[ENG-201-R001] Controlled CI, SBOM/provenance, dependency/license/vulnerability review, secret scanning, SAST/SCA/IaC and artifact integrity.
[ENG-201-R002] C4/C5 releases include exact commit/tag, migrations, Clinical BOM, tests, security evidence, algorithm/knowledge diffs, AI evals, approvals, rollout and reconciliation plan.
[ENG-201-R003] Direct production DB/prompt/knowledge/algorithm changes are prohibited outside governed emergency procedure.
ENG-202 | ACTIVE | 202. Clinical safety case and hazard control hierarchy
[ENG-202-R001] Each C4/C5 capability maintains claim → argument → evidence for intended use, hazards, controls, verification, clinician validation, residual risk and monitoring.
[ENG-202-R002] Prefer elimination by design, deterministic prevention, detection/reconciliation, then warnings/training.
[ENG-202-R003] A warning banner is not the first solution to a preventable architecture defect.
ENG-203 | ACTIVE | 203. Risk classes and maturity
[ENG-203-R001] Engineering risk classes: C0 cosmetic, C1 administrative, C2 workflow, C3 clinical informational, C4 decision influence, C5 safety critical. These are engineering governance classes, not regulatory classification.
[ENG-203-R002] Capability maturity: M0 Concept → M1 Specified → M2 Implemented → M3 Verified → M4 Clinically validated → M5 Production hardened → M6 Continuously assured.
[ENG-203-R003] Production admission depends on risk-specific maturity and current evidence.
ENG-204 | ACTIVE | 204. Synthetic validation versus real clinical validation
[ENG-204-R001] 2,000 synthetic physicians, millions of synthetic patients or large generated test corpora cannot replace representative real-clinician validation.
[ENG-204-R002] Synthetic testing is for hypothesis discovery, adversarial coverage, performance and regression.
[ENG-204-R003] Clinical validity, usability and intended-use safety require qualified real-world review appropriate to risk.
ENG-205 | ACTIVE | 205. No complexity, scale, security or compliance theater
[ENG-205-R001] Do not add microservices, event sourcing, graph/vector databases, DSLs or formal methods without a measured problem and ADR.
[ENG-205-R002] Do not size architecture from theoretical variable count; size real entities/cardinalities/event rates/query patterns while preserving scale escape hatches.
[ENG-205-R003] Encryption labels/framework mappings are not security evidence.
[ENG-205-R004] Compliance mappings/checklists are not certification or legal conclusion.
ENG-206 | ACTIVE | 206. Root AGENTS.md and agent execution separation
[ENG-206-R001] This engineering specification is authoritative engineering source, not root agent prompt.
[ENG-206-R002] Root AGENTS.md should remain concise and route agents to scoped authoritative documents.
[ENG-206-R003] Agent task packets contain issue, requirements, risk, scope, invariants, forbidden changes, tests, evidence and stop conditions.
[ENG-206-R004] Agent-generated C4/C5 code/content is untrusted until required independent verification passes.
ENG-207 | ACTIVE | 207. Stop-the-line conditions
[ENG-207-R001] Cross-tenant PHI exposure.
[ENG-207-R002] Wrong-patient high-impact mutation.
[ENG-207-R003] Signed-record corruption.
[ENG-207-R004] Dangerous deterministic calculation discrepancy.
[ENG-207-R005] Silent critical-result/follow-up loss.
[ENG-207-R006] Unreviewed clinically consequential behavior.
[ENG-207-R007] Unknown units or applicability in a safety-critical path.
[ENG-207-R008] Audit/authorization bypass.
[ENG-207-R009] Autonomous irreversible AI outside approved intended use.
[ENG-207-R010] Failed critical invariant or inability to reconstruct a high-impact action.
ENG-208 | ACTIVE | 208. V2 ↔ V2.1.1 traceability extension
[ENG-208-R001] The existing V2 traceability matrix remains authoritative. This amendment adds cross-cutting engineering obligations that attach to multiple V2 requirements rather than changing their product intent.
V2.1.1 area

Evidence gate
Algorithm Platform

Manifest + golden/property/boundary tests + clinical review
Knowledge Engine
