ADRs, invariants, state machines, runbooks, hazards, algorithm manifests, knowledge artifacts and postmortems are versioned and searchable. Chat history is not the system of record.
1593. ROOT AGENTS.MD SIZE CONTROL
Do not place this entire specification into root AGENTS.md. Root agent instructions remain concise and point to authoritative product, engineering, safety, computation and domain documents by scope.
1594. AGENT TASK PACKET
Every coding-agent task includes issue ID, authoritative requirements, risk class, scope, files, invariants, forbidden changes, test plan, expected evidence and stop conditions.
1595. AGENT CONTEXT MINIMIZATION
Give coding agents the smallest authoritative context that preserves correctness. Huge undifferentiated prompts increase contradiction and omission risk.
1596. AGENT GENERATED CODE TRUST
Agent code is untrusted contribution until deterministic tests, static analysis, independent review and required clinical/security evidence pass.
1597. AGENT CLINICAL CONTENT PROHIBITION
Coding agents may scaffold algorithms/knowledge structures but cannot invent clinical thresholds, contraindications, dosing rules or guideline recommendations. Such content requires approved sources and clinical governance.
1598. AGENT SECURITY PROHIBITION
Agents cannot weaken RLS/authz, disable tests, add universal admin bypasses, expose secrets/PHI or change production security posture merely to make a task pass.
1599. AGENT STOP CONDITIONS 2.0
Stop on conflicting authoritative specs, unknown high-impact clinical behavior, uncertain units, unclear intended use, destructive migration, cross-tenant risk, audit bypass, unowned obligation/result, autonomous irreversible AI, unreviewed clinical content or failing critical invariant.
1600. REQUIREMENT ATOMICITY
Break giant requirements into atomic statements with ID, rationale, risk, acceptance, owner and traceability. One paragraph containing twenty obligations is not one testable requirement.
1601. INVARIANT ATOMICITY
Each invariant has scope, formal-ish statement, prevention, detection, recovery and tests. Avoid slogans without executable consequence.
1602. ACCEPTANCE EVIDENCE
Acceptance can require automated tests, screenshots, trace samples, migration output, clinical review, performance result, security report, restore evidence or usability result depending on risk.
1603. EVIDENCE EXPIRATION
Some evidence expires: penetration test, restore drill, AI evaluation, knowledge freshness and performance benchmarks. M6 status requires current evidence.
1604. ASSURANCE DASHBOARD
Show maturity and evidence freshness by capability/risk. Green means current evidence satisfies defined gate—not “no one reported a problem.”
1605. RISK-BASED RELEASE AUTHORITY
C0/C1 may use normal peer review; C2/C3 add domain/QA; C4 adds clinical/safety; C5 requires strongest independent clinical, numerical/security/reliability evidence appropriate to function.
1606. PHYSICIAN OVERRIDE ANALYSIS
High override rates can mean poor rule, wrong applicability, workflow mismatch or appropriate clinician judgment. Analyze context before changing thresholds.
1607. PHYSICIAN WORKAROUND DETECTION
Repeated copying to notes, external calculators, screenshots, paper lists or manual reminders indicate product failure or trust gap and enter product investigation.
1608. PHYSICIAN TIME BUDGET
For each encounter stage define target interaction budget and identify system-caused time. Do not optimize by removing clinically necessary review or making hidden defaults.
1609. DATA ONCE PRINCIPLE
If trustworthy data already exists and remains applicable, reuse it with provenance; do not ask again merely because another module owns a screen. Reconfirmation is explicit where clinically necessary.
1610. COMPUTE ONCE / VERIFY MANY
Authoritative deterministic derived facts are computed by one governed service/version and reused; independent implementations exist for validation, not competing production answers.
1611. SOURCE ONCE / INTERPRET VERSIONED
Immutable source observations/documents remain preserved while normalization, interpretation and knowledge layers can evolve versionedly.
1612. CLINICAL STATE IN SECONDS
Patient Workspace prioritizes a bounded high-signal state summary generated from structured longitudinal data with links to source evidence. It must not require scanning the entire chart.
1613. EXPLANATION ON DEMAND
High-impact algorithm/knowledge/AI outputs expose concise rationale first and deeper provenance on demand, avoiding both opaque automation and overwhelming default detail.
1614. NO AI THEATER
Do not add AI badges, chat panels or generated prose where deterministic state, search or well-designed UI solves the task better.
1615. NO COMPLEXITY THEATER
Do not add microservices, graph databases, vector databases, event sourcing, formal methods or custom DSL merely because the system is ambitious. Each must solve a measured problem with lower total risk than alternatives.
1616. NO SCALE THEATER
Do not design for impossible theoretical combinations at the expense of real workload. Preserve scale escape hatches through contracts, partition keys and stateless services, then benchmark actual cardinalities.
1617. NO SECURITY THEATER
Encryption labels and framework names are not security. Require threat model, key lifecycle, authorization, testing, monitoring, incident response and evidence.
1618. NO COMPLIANCE THEATER
A checklist or standard mapping is not certification/compliance. Maintain requirement→control→evidence→test→owner and obtain qualified legal/regulatory assessment for target jurisdictions.
1619. NO VALIDATION THEATER
2,000 synthetic doctors, millions of synthetic patients or billions of generated tests cannot replace real clinician validation, representative data, independent review and production monitoring.
1620. SYSTEM OF SYSTEMS VIEW
Medical OS comprises interacting clinical, workflow, knowledge, AI, security, identity, data, interoperability, communication and operational systems. Optimize local modules only within global invariants.
1621. GLOBAL INVARIANT CATALOG
At minimum: tenant isolation; patient-context binding; signed immutability; provenance preservation; no silent unknown→negative; no silent AI authority escalation; no orphan critical work; deterministic safety core; idempotent external effects; reconstructable high-impact decisions.
1622. GLOBAL RECOVERY CATALOG
For each invariant define how the system detects historical violations and repairs/contains them. Prevention without retrospective detection is incomplete.
1623. SYSTEM-WIDE KILL SWITCHES
Provide scoped disable controls for AI task, knowledge pack, algorithm version, integration, communication channel and feature. Kill switches are tested, audited and designed not to disable unrelated clinical core.
1624. SAFE MODE
Define a reduced-capability mode preserving patient lookup, chart read, critical work visibility, basic encounter documentation, deterministic core and audit during selected dependency failures.
1625. DOWNTIME PROCEDURE
Document and rehearse what clinicians do if core platform is unavailable, how temporary records are protected and how they reconcile after restoration. Software architecture includes operational continuity.
1626. RECOVERY PRIORITY
Restore identity/authz, chart/source truth, critical results/obligations, audit/workflows, documents, projections/search, integrations and secondary AI/analytics according to risk—not cosmetic dependency order.
1627. 150-EXPERT PARALLELIZATION LAW
Parallelize implementation only after shared semantic kernel, contracts, invariants, repository boundaries and test harness exist. Otherwise 150 experts create 150 incompatible interpretations faster.
1628. TEAM INTERFACE CONTRACTS
Each team publishes APIs/events/schema, ownership, invariants, SLO, failure semantics and version policy. Cross-team dependencies use contracts, not tribal knowledge.
1629. ARCHITECTURE REVIEW CADENCE
Weekly domain reviews, biweekly cross-domain hazard review, release-specific C4/C5 councils, monthly reliability/security evidence review and periodic architecture simplification review.
1630. SIMPLIFICATION REVIEW
Every quarter identify abstractions, flags, adapters, services and rules that can be removed without reducing capability. Sophistication includes deleting unnecessary complexity.
1631. TECHNICAL DEBT RISK MODEL
Debt is tagged by safety, security, reliability, scale, maintainability and velocity impact. High-risk debt gets explicit owner/deadline and cannot hide as generic backlog.
1632. DEPRECATION
APIs, algorithms, knowledge artifacts, terminology and UI flows have deprecation periods, telemetry and migration paths. Historical reproducibility survives retirement.
1633. VERSION VECTOR
A high-impact output can be reconstructed from a compact version vector: app, schema, algorithm, knowledge, terminology, AI task/model config, country/specialty pack and relevant policy.
1634. CLINICAL ACTION RECEIPT
For selected high-impact actions create an internal receipt containing actor, patient, encounter, intent, source facts, safety checks, versions, outcome, side effects and audit references.
1635. PATIENT IMPACT QUERY
Given incident/version/artifact/model/task, efficiently identify potentially affected patients/actions without scanning arbitrary unindexed blobs.
1636. DATA LINEAGE QUERY
Given a displayed fact/recommendation, traverse backward to source and forward to dependent calculations/alerts/obligations/actions, subject to authorization.
1637. REPRODUCIBLE CLINICAL REPLAY
For incident analysis, replay deterministic computation/knowledge using historical version vector and source facts. AI replay is not assumed deterministic; preserve request/config/output evidence subject to privacy policy.
1638. AI REPRODUCIBILITY LIMIT
Record enough model/task/config/context/evidence metadata for investigation, but do not falsely promise byte-identical reproduction of stochastic external models.
1639. KNOWLEDGE REPRODUCIBILITY
Historical approved knowledge packages remain content-addressable and executable in isolated replay environment even after supersession, subject to licensing/retention.
1640. ALGORITHM REPRODUCIBILITY
Historical algorithm versions remain buildable/testable or have preserved executable artifact/source and manifest sufficient for audit/replay.
1641. DATABASE REPRODUCIBILITY
Migration chain and schema snapshots permit reconstructing relevant historical schema for audit/test. Never rely solely on current ORM models to interpret old records.
1642. DESIGN REPRODUCIBILITY
For high-impact UX incidents retain versioned design/component behavior and visual/semantic regression artifacts sufficient to understand what clinician saw.
1643. 30-CYCLE EXIT CRITERIA
The 30-pass circuit can exit only when:
no unresolved S0/S1;
all C5 global invariants have prevention + detection + recovery + evidence;
S2 risks are explicitly governed;
all new architectural assumptions are registered;
affected passes were rerun after major fixes;
real-clinician validation plan exists for C4/C5;
residual risk is explicit.
1644. WHAT 100% MEANS
“100%” cannot mean all theoretical clinical states are proven safe. In this architecture it means 100% of declared critical invariants/reference cases/gates for the defined intended use pass, with known residual risks documented. Never convert that into a universal safety claim.
1645. THE MONSTER SYSTEM RULE
The system may be enormous in capability but must remain small in each local reasoning boundary. A developer should reason about one aggregate, one state machine, one algorithm or one workflow without loading the entire platform into memory.
1646. THE NANOMETER-BEYOND-NANOMETER RULE
When a requirement says “every letter, button, action, database, link, security and encryption,” translate ambition into machine-verifiable contracts: semantic UI contracts, typed domain contracts, data contracts, authz policies, cryptographic key lifecycle, workflow state machines, observability, reconciliation and evidence. Precision comes from explicitness, not document length alone.
1647. FINAL HYPERSCALE PRINCIPLE
Medical OS should behave as a clinically governed distributed system whose complexity is decomposed into bounded, testable, reconstructable units. The architecture must remain safe when AI is wrong, a provider is down, a message is duplicated, a clinician is interrupted, a database transaction conflicts, a guideline changes, a patient is switched, a result is corrected, a key rotates or a deployment partially fails.
1648. FINAL 150-EXPERT CONSTITUTION
The target is not a “perfect document.” The target is an executable architecture in which every high-impact behavior has:
**owner + authority + source + semantics + state + invariant + implementation boundary + test oracle + failure policy + recovery + telemetry + version + audit + evidence + human governance.**
Only then does complexity become controlled capability instead of uncontrolled risk.

V2.1.1 — ULTRA-SENIOR TARGET ARCHITECTURE EXPANSION
Purpose. This expansion translates the newly defined Medical OS target into engineering architecture without silently changing the frozen V2 product baseline. Capabilities that alter physician-visible product behavior are marked PRODUCT-GAP and require promotion into the next governed V2 product revision before unrestricted production activation.
ENG-212 | ACTIVE | 212. Target architecture doctrine
[ENG-212-R001] Medical OS shall evolve from an advanced EHR into a longitudinal computable clinical operating system.
[ENG-212-R002] The core loop is Patient State → Intent → Goals → Problems/Hypotheses → Evidence/Uncertainty → Decision → Intervention → Expected Result → Observed Result → Treatment Response → Reassessment → Obligation.
[ENG-212-R003] Every new capability must reduce cognitive load, preserve clinical continuity, improve safety, close work loops, or make uncertainty/intent explicit.
[ENG-212-R004] Generative AI is assistive; deterministic computation, governed knowledge and physician authority remain separate authorities.
ENG-213 | ACTIVE | 213. Patient Computable State 2.0
[ENG-213-R001] Extend Patient State to include current state, historical state, delta, trajectory, episodes, problems, hypotheses, evidence, uncertainty, goals, intent, interventions, treatment responses, medications, diagnostics, preferences, risks, care gaps, obligations, expected results, contradictions, decisions and provenance.
[ENG-213-R002] Implement as composable domain objects/read models, never as one patient_digital_twin mega-table.
[ENG-213-R003] Every state projection exposes as-of time/version, freshness, completeness, degraded dependencies and provenance.
ENG-214 | ACTIVE | 214. Clinical Intent as a first-class domain primitive
[ENG-214-R001] ClinicalIntent captures why an action is being performed: purpose, target problem/goal, expected information/effect, initiating clinician, encounter/context, temporal horizon and provenance.
[ENG-214-R002] Orders, interventions, referrals, monitoring and selected decisions may reference ClinicalIntent.
[ENG-214-R003] AI may propose intent only as DRAFT/UNVERIFIED; it cannot silently infer and commit physician intent.
[ENG-214-R004] PRODUCT-GAP: physician-visible intent capture/review behavior requires product-baseline promotion.
ENG-215 | ACTIVE | 215. Clinical Goal Engine
[ENG-215-R001] ClinicalGoal represents desired clinical outcome, target semantics, measurement method, horizon, owner, lifecycle and relationship to problems/interventions.
[ENG-215-R002] Lifecycle supports proposed, active, achieved, partially-achieved, not-achieved, superseded, cancelled and unable-to-assess.
[ENG-215-R003] Goal evaluation distinguishes population guideline target, individualized target and patient preference.
[ENG-215-R004] Goal closure requires evidence or explicit clinician action; encounter closure cannot auto-close a longitudinal goal.
ENG-216 | ACTIVE | 216. Clinical Plan Compiler
[ENG-216-R001] Compile an approved clinical plan into typed downstream work: prescription candidates, orders, monitoring requirements, expected results, obligations, referrals, follow-up, patient instructions and communications.
[ENG-216-R002] Decision Once → Propagate Everywhere is a target invariant.
[ENG-216-R003] Compilation produces a preview/diff before physician commit for clinically consequential outputs.
[ENG-216-R004] Partial compilation failure is explicit; successfully created children and failed children are reconciled, never silently orphaned.
ENG-217 | ACTIVE | 217. Expected Result Engine
[ENG-217-R001] Every eligible diagnostic/monitoring order may define expected artifact/result type, expected time window, responsible reviewer, escalation policy and downstream obligation.
[ENG-217-R002] Absence of an expected result becomes a detectable workflow state rather than disappearing.
[ENG-217-R003] Expected result is distinct from predicted biological value unless an explicitly validated model exists.
[ENG-217-R004] Corrections/amendments can invalidate prior review and reopen downstream obligations.
ENG-218 | ACTIVE | 218. Clinical Goal-to-Result closure model
[ENG-218-R001] Link Goal → Intervention → Expected Result → Observed Result → Treatment Response → Reassessment.
[ENG-218-R002] Support one-to-many and many-to-one relationships without forcing false causality.
[ENG-218-R003] Unknown response remains UNKNOWN; missing follow-up cannot become treatment failure or success.
[ENG-218-R004] Historical reconstruction must show which evidence was available when response was assessed.
ENG-219 | ACTIVE | 219. Treatment Response Engine
[ENG-219-R001] Represent expected effect, expected timeframe, observed response, adverse effects, adherence evidence, confounders and clinician conclusion.
[ENG-219-R002] Outcome states include responding, partially responding, non-responding, worsening, unable-to-assess and conflicting evidence.
[ENG-219-R003] Deterministic response rules may be used only when clinically validated; otherwise the engine structures evidence for clinician assessment.
[ENG-219-R004] PRODUCT-GAP: visible response classifications and workflow require product review.
ENG-220 | ACTIVE | 220. Medication Intelligence Graph 2.0
[ENG-220-R001] Connect medication to indication, problem, goal, intent, dose, route, formulation, start/stop, response, adverse effects, allergies, contraindications, renal/hepatic context, monitoring, adherence and reason for change.
[ENG-220-R002] Detect medication-without-known-indication, indication-without-expected-monitoring, monitoring overdue and clinically meaningful lifecycle inconsistencies as review candidates.
[ENG-220-R003] Do not equate absence of linked indication with inappropriate prescribing; imported/legacy data may be incomplete.
ENG-221 | ACTIVE | 221. Medication Change Impact Engine
[ENG-221-R001] When renal/hepatic function, weight, pregnancy/context, allergy, interaction-relevant medication or other validated dependency changes, recompute only affected medication safety/applicability checks.
[ENG-221-R002] Display delta rather than re-alerting unchanged findings.
[ENG-221-R003] All high-impact medication checks declare algorithm/knowledge versions and missing-data behavior.
ENG-222 | ACTIVE | 222. Hypothesis Workspace
[ENG-222-R001] ClinicalHypothesis stores status, supporting evidence, contradicting evidence, missing evidence, uncertainty, differential relationships, risk-of-omission metadata and provenance.
[ENG-222-R002] Lifecycle may include considered, evaluating, probable, confirmed, ruled-out and superseded, subject to specialty/product governance.
[ENG-222-R003] Promotion to diagnosis is an explicit physician action unless a separately validated/regulatory-approved pathway permits otherwise.
[ENG-222-R004] PRODUCT-GAP: hypothesis UI and lifecycle must be added to product specification.
ENG-223 | ACTIVE | 223. Missing Evidence Engine
[ENG-223-R001] For a hypothesis/decision, compute what required or useful evidence is absent, unknown, conflicting or stale.
[ENG-223-R002] Separate MUST-HAVE safety data from optional decision-improving information.
[ENG-223-R003] Do not create checklist overload; rank by expected decision relevance, urgency and risk.
[ENG-223-R004] Knowledge-derived missing-evidence logic is versioned and explainable.
ENG-224 | ACTIVE | 224. Clinical Contradiction Engine
[ENG-224-R001] Detect semantic conflicts across facts, statuses, medications, problems, results, statements and timelines.
[ENG-224-R002] Contradiction states include possible, likely, clinically-important and safety-critical; confidence never substitutes for clinical severity.
[ENG-224-R003] Examples include active-vs-stopped medication, incompatible units/statuses, documented absence versus persistent objective evidence and conflicting patient identity/context.
[ENG-224-R004] Contradictions require reconciliation workflow; the system does not silently choose a winner.
ENG-225 | ACTIVE | 225. Concordance and discrepancy graph
[ENG-225-R001] Represent agreements and disagreements between structured facts, documents, clinician statements, patient statements, imported data and derived facts.
[ENG-225-R002] Source authority, recency and verification are inputs to reconciliation but never erase provenance.
[ENG-225-R003] Conflicting facts remain separately reconstructable after reconciliation.
ENG-226 | ACTIVE | 226. Clinical Uncertainty object
[ENG-226-R001] Uncertainty is first-class and can attach to hypothesis, diagnosis, fact, decision, expected result or response.
[ENG-226-R002] States distinguish unknown, uncertain, conflicting, pending, not-assessed and not-applicable.
[ENG-226-R003] Store uncertainty rationale and what evidence could resolve it when explicitly known.
[ENG-226-R004] Never fabricate numerical probability where no validated probability model exists.
ENG-227 | ACTIVE | 227. Clinical Decision object and Decision Ledger
[ENG-227-R001] Selected high-impact decisions may capture decision, intent, alternatives considered, evidence, constraints, uncertainty, expected outcome, monitoring plan and actor/time.
[ENG-227-R002] Decision Ledger is risk-based; routine low-risk care must not become documentation burden.
[ENG-227-R003] AI-generated decision rationale remains draft until physician adoption.
ENG-228 | ACTIVE | 228. Counterfactual / Value-of-Information boundary
[ENG-228-R001] Provide a governed framework to represent whether additional information could change a decision.
[ENG-228-R002] Do not claim causal counterfactual prediction unless a validated model and intended use support it.
[ENG-228-R003] Initial implementation should structure decision branches and missing information, not autonomously optimize clinical testing.
[ENG-228-R004] C4/C5 deployment requires clinical validation and function-specific regulatory review.
ENG-229 | ACTIVE | 229. Clinical Delta Engine
[ENG-229-R001] Compute what changed since a clinically relevant baseline/last review: new problems, changed measurements, medication changes, new results, corrected results, new contradictions, overdue obligations and resolved items.
[ENG-229-R002] Rank by clinical relevance and novelty rather than raw chronology.
[ENG-229-R003] Every delta links to underlying facts and comparison baseline.
ENG-230 | ACTIVE | 230. What Changed? pre-visit contract
[ENG-230-R001] Pre-visit view must prioritize meaningful deltas, pending work, contradictions, new external care and treatment-response evidence.
[ENG-230-R002] Long AI summaries cannot replace structured delta.
[ENG-230-R003] User can drill from delta → source → longitudinal context.
ENG-231 | ACTIVE | 231. Semantic Clinical Timeline
[ENG-231-R001] Support chronological, problem-centric, medication-centric, diagnostic and episode-centric projections over the same authoritative facts.
[ENG-231-R002] Timeline grouping is a read model; it does not duplicate clinical truth.
[ENG-231-R003] Episode assignment can be suggested by AI but remains provenance-marked and correctable.
ENG-232 | ACTIVE | 232. Clinical Episode model
[ENG-232-R001] ClinicalEpisode groups temporally/semantically related encounters, diagnostics, interventions and outcomes.
[ENG-232-R002] Episode boundaries may be explicit, deterministic or suggested; source/method is preserved.
[ENG-232-R003] One fact may participate in multiple clinically meaningful views without being copied.
ENG-233 | ACTIVE | 233. Patient Preference and Shared Decision model
[ENG-233-R001] Represent preferences, constraints, affordability/access considerations, prior intolerance and feasibility separately from clinical facts.
[ENG-233-R002] Preferences have scope, source, effective period and verification state.
[ENG-233-R003] Shared decisions link clinical options, patient constraints/preferences and final physician/patient decision without pretending guideline equivalence.
ENG-234 | ACTIVE | 234. Clinical Memory architecture
[ENG-234-R001] Separate durable clinical facts, patient preferences, prior decisions, historical context, clinician annotations and AI inferences.
[ENG-234-R002] AI memory never silently becomes clinical truth.
[ENG-234-R003] Memory retrieval is purpose-scoped and minimum necessary.
ENG-235 | ACTIVE | 235. Attention Budget Engine
[ENG-235-R001] Alerts/signals are assigned interruption class: CRITICAL, IMPORTANT, CONSIDER or INFO under governed policy.
[ENG-235-R002] Correlated rules about the same underlying hazard should be deduplicated/aggregated where safe.
[ENG-235-R003] Track alert exposure, acknowledgement, override and downstream outcome for governance without using raw ignore rate as automatic clinical truth.
[ENG-235-R004] Critical alerts cannot be suppressed solely because users frequently dismiss them.
ENG-236 | ACTIVE | 236. Cognitive Load Controller
[ENG-236-R001] Instrument clicks, repeated entry, context switches, interruptions, search time, scroll distance, alert burden and after-visit work using privacy-preserving telemetry.
[ENG-236-R002] Use metrics to identify workflow friction, not to score/punish clinicians by default.
[ENG-236-R003] Product optimization must preserve safety invariants while reducing interaction cost.
ENG-237 | ACTIVE | 237. Ambient Clinical Layer 2.0
[ENG-237-R001] Conversation/audio may generate candidate history facts, exam statements, medications, orders, instructions, obligations and missing-question suggestions.
[ENG-237-R002] Pipeline: capture → segmentation → extraction → context binding → contradiction check → candidate objects → physician review → commit.
[ENG-237-R003] Ambient extraction cannot directly create verified clinical facts or irreversible clinical actions.
[ENG-237-R004] Late/stale ambient outputs are patient/encounter/request bound and safely discarded.
ENG-238 | ACTIVE | 238. Ambient Missing Question Detection
[ENG-238-R001] During encounter, surface potentially important unanswered questions only when supported by approved clinical knowledge/pathway and current context.
[ENG-238-R002] Use progressive disclosure and attention budget; do not continuously interrupt.
[ENG-238-R003] Each suggestion explains why it may matter and can be dismissed without silently recording a negative answer.
ENG-239 | ACTIVE | 239. Evidence-at-the-point-of-decision
[ENG-239-R001] Resolve applicable evidence using jurisdiction, specialty, life stage, condition, medication/context, effective date and artifact version.
[ENG-239-R002] Display recommendation, source, effective date, applicability, exceptions, strength/quality metadata where available and data used.
[ENG-239-R003] Knowledge conflict is explicit; AI cannot invent consensus.
ENG-240 | ACTIVE | 240. Explain Why contract
[ENG-240-R001] Every high-impact deterministic/knowledge recommendation exposes trigger facts, missing facts, rule/algorithm version, source references, applicability and reason for severity.
[ENG-240-R002] Explanation must be generated from execution trace where possible, not reconstructed by a free-form LLM.
[ENG-240-R003] AI prose may translate an existing trace but cannot change its semantics.
ENG-241 | ACTIVE | 241. Patient Digital Twin clinical composition
[ENG-241-R001] Patient Digital Twin is the composed longitudinal experience over Patient State, Graph, Delta, Trajectory, Intent, Goals, Hypotheses, Interventions, Responses and Obligations.
[ENG-241-R002] It is not a claim of complete physiological simulation or future prediction.
[ENG-241-R003] Simulation/prediction modules are separately versioned capabilities with explicit uncertainty and validation.
ENG-242 | ACTIVE | 242. Patient Action Plan / Patient OS 2.0
[ENG-242-R001] Translate approved care plan into patient-facing actions: what, when, why, preparation, completion state, contact/escalation and barriers.
[ENG-242-R002] Patient-facing explanations are separated from clinician-facing clinical reasoning.
[ENG-242-R003] Patient responses may create messages/candidate updates/obligations but cannot overwrite clinician-verified facts.
ENG-243 | ACTIVE | 243. Patient Comprehension and Barrier Loop
[ENG-243-R001] Support comprehension checks and reporting of barriers such as cost, access, scheduling or intolerance.
[ENG-243-R002] Barrier data is patient-reported unless verified otherwise.
[ENG-243-R003] Clinically important barriers route to an owner/obligation instead of disappearing into messages.
ENG-244 | ACTIVE | 244. Care Team Operating System
[ENG-244-R001] Model physician, nurse, assistant, reception, laboratory, pharmacy, care coordinator, specialist, patient and caregiver responsibilities through scoped roles/capabilities.
[ENG-244-R002] Work is routed by responsibility, relationship, purpose and urgency.
[ENG-244-R003] No task can become ownerless because a staff member is deactivated; reassignment/reconciliation is mandatory.
ENG-245 | ACTIVE | 245. Referral Intelligence 2.0
[ENG-245-R001] Referral captures reason, urgency, clinical question, relevant evidence, medications, requested output and expected return state.
[ENG-245-R002] Incoming specialist response triggers reconciliation of diagnoses, medications, recommendations, results and obligations.
[ENG-245-R003] Referral sent ≠ accepted ≠ completed ≠ reconciled.
ENG-246 | ACTIVE | 246. Message Intelligence
[ENG-246-R001] Classify message intent/urgency/context, propose routing and draft response, and create obligation when appropriate.
[ENG-246-R002] AI classification cannot suppress or close potentially urgent clinical messages without validated deterministic safety controls/human review.
[ENG-246-R003] Message state includes received, triaged, assigned, responded, awaiting-patient, escalated and closed.
ENG-247 | ACTIVE | 247. Operational Intelligence
[ENG-247-R001] Command Center should surface clinical-operational work: critical/unreviewed results, overdue obligations, referral loops, failed communications, no-shows needing action, duplicate-patient candidates and degraded services.
[ENG-247-R002] Operational prioritization must not obscure clinical severity.
ENG-248 | ACTIVE | 248. Autonomous Administrative Plane
[ENG-248-R001] Permit higher automation for low-risk scheduling, reminders, document preparation, routing and administrative reconciliation under explicit capabilities.
[ENG-248-R002] Autonomy decreases as clinical consequence rises.
[ENG-248-R003] Administrative automation cannot silently mutate diagnoses, prescriptions, verified clinical facts or critical-result closure.
ENG-249 | ACTIVE | 249. Clinical Quality Observatory
[ENG-249-R001] Measure closed-loop completion, monitoring gaps, result review, referral closure, care-gap resolution, alert effectiveness, documentation completeness and workflow burden.
[ENG-249-R002] Quality metrics preserve denominator/version definitions and are not silently reinterpreted after knowledge updates.
[ENG-249-R003] Separate quality improvement analytics from punitive individual surveillance by policy.
ENG-250 | ACTIVE | 250. Learning Health System firewall
[ENG-250-R001] Production behavior never self-modifies from usage telemetry.
[ENG-250-R002] Telemetry may open governance hypotheses: poor applicability, alert burden, workflow friction, model drift or knowledge gaps.
[ENG-250-R003] Any change proceeds through source/review/validation/shadow/canary/release.
[ENG-250-R004] Clinician override is evidence for review, not automatic proof that a rule is wrong.
ENG-251 | ACTIVE | 251. Outcome Intelligence boundary

[ENG-251-R002] Do not present observational association as causal treatment effect.

[ENG-252-R002] Output is potential-match with satisfied/unsatisfied/unknown criteria and source version, never automatic enrollment.

ENG-253 | ACTIVE | 253. Federated analytics readiness

ENG-254 | ACTIVE | 254. Clinical Simulation Mode

[ENG-254-R002] Report trigger counts, changed populations, severity changes, workload/alert burden and unresolved cases.

ENG-255 | FUTURE | 255. Institutional Digital Twin boundary
[ENG-255-R001] Future operational simulation may model demand, staffing, scheduling, labs, messaging, inventory and throughput.
[ENG-255-R002] Operational simulation is separate from patient clinical truth and cannot silently drive clinical prioritization.
[ENG-255-R003] Architecture should allow it without coupling core clinical domain to simulation.
ENG-256 | ACTIVE | 256. Computable care-plan graph
[ENG-256-R001] Create a typed graph connecting Intent → Goal → Decision → Intervention → Expected Result → Observation → Response → Obligation.
[ENG-256-R002] Graph edges have semantic type, source, actor, effective time, recorded time and lifecycle.
[ENG-256-R003] Missing edge is not inferred as negative relationship.
ENG-257 | ACTIVE | 257. Plan compilation transaction semantics
[ENG-257-R001] Plan compilation creates an immutable proposal graph before commit.
[ENG-257-R002] Commit validates authorization, patient context, current versions, dependencies and duplicate/idempotency keys.

[ENG-258-R001] Intent, Goal, Hypothesis, Decision, Intervention, ExpectedResult, TreatmentResponse, Episode and Preference require stable opaque IDs and append-oriented history.

ENG-259 | ACTIVE | 259. New object information-state policy
[ENG-259-R001] Every new domain primitive defines behavior for unknown, unverified, conflicting, pending, not-assessed and not-applicable states where semantically relevant.
[ENG-259-R002] Null is not a universal information state.

ENG-260 | ACTIVE | 260. New object provenance policy
[ENG-260-R001] Every clinically meaningful object stores source type, actor/system, source artifact/reference, creation/effective time, verification state and transformation lineage where derived.
[ENG-260-R002] AI-generated candidate objects identify task/model/config and source spans when available.

ENG-261 | ACTIVE | 261. New object authorization policy
[ENG-261-R001] Authorization is object/action/purpose scoped and evaluated server-side.
[ENG-261-R002] Patient preference, sensitive history, research data and caregiver access may require additional policy dimensions.
[ENG-261-R003] Background jobs re-establish authorization/service capability rather than inheriting stale request context.
ENG-262 | ACTIVE | 262. New object audit policy
[ENG-262-R001] Create/update/supersede/verify/commit/close/reopen/export and high-impact read events are audited according to policy.
[ENG-262-R002] Audit must distinguish AI proposal, physician acceptance and final committed state.
[ENG-262-R003] Audit failure policy is explicit for clinically consequential writes.
ENG-263 | ACTIVE | 263. New object search/index policy
[ENG-263-R001] Search indexes may expose safe summaries but never become source of truth or authorization authority.
[ENG-263-R002] New clinical primitives are searchable only where purpose and sensitivity permit.
[ENG-263-R003] Index lag/failure is visible and cannot silently hide critical obligations/results.
ENG-264 | ACTIVE | 264. New object reconciliation policy
[ENG-264-R001] Reconciliation jobs detect orphan goals, interventions without expected monitoring, expected results without returned result, responses without source evidence, obligations without owner and stale projections.
[ENG-264-R002] Reconciliation can create repair work but does not invent missing clinical facts.
ENG-265 | ACTIVE | 265. New object deletion/retention policy
[ENG-265-R001] Clinical semantics use explicit lifecycle/supersession rather than generic soft delete.
[ENG-265-R002] Retention and correction behavior are jurisdiction/purpose governed.
[ENG-265-R003] Deleting a projection/cache never deletes authoritative clinical history.
ENG-266 | ACTIVE | 266. AI contracts for new reasoning objects
[ENG-266-R001] AI can summarize, extract, propose links, propose hypotheses, identify candidate contradictions and draft explanations within task-specific schemas.
[ENG-266-R002] AI cannot be authoritative source for dose arithmetic, unit conversion, authorization, signed state, critical-result closure or verified clinical fact.
[ENG-266-R003] All high-impact AI outputs support abstention and source/evidence trace.
ENG-267 | ACTIVE | 267. Deterministic contracts for new reasoning objects
[ENG-267-R001] Use deterministic logic for lifecycle transitions, due dates, unit/temporal calculations, dependency invalidation, duplicate detection primitives and validated safety rules.
[ENG-267-R002] Clinical thresholds and applicability rules are knowledge/algorithm artifacts, not hard-coded magic numbers.
[ENG-267-R003] Unknown input propagates explicitly.
ENG-268 | ACTIVE | 268. Knowledge Engine extensions for goals and care plans
[ENG-268-R001] Knowledge artifacts may define recommended goals, monitoring, expected follow-up intervals, evidence requirements and care-plan relationships.
[ENG-268-R002] Recommendations remain context/jurisdiction/version dependent.
[ENG-268-R003] Knowledge cannot silently individualize a target without required patient/context inputs and physician authority.
ENG-269 | ACTIVE | 269. Knowledge Engine extensions for contradictions
[ENG-269-R001] Contradiction rules distinguish logical impossibility, semantic inconsistency, temporal mismatch and clinically suspicious coexistence.
[ENG-269-R002] Each rule declares severity, applicability, resolution options and false-positive considerations.
[ENG-269-R003] Rule firing creates review signal, not automatic fact deletion.
ENG-270 | ACTIVE | 270. Knowledge Engine extensions for missing evidence
[ENG-270-R001] Represent required, recommended and optional evidence separately.
[ENG-270-R002] Missing-evidence artifacts declare decision/hypothesis context and applicability.
[ENG-270-R003] Do not equate absence from Medical OS with evidence that assessment was not performed; source completeness matters.
ENG-271 | ACTIVE | 271. Longitudinal causality guardrail
[ENG-271-R001] Temporal sequence, correlation and response after intervention do not automatically establish causality.
[ENG-271-R002] Treatment Response may record clinician-attributed relationship separately from observed temporal association.
[ENG-271-R003] AI explanations must not upgrade association to causation.
ENG-272 | ACTIVE | 272. Data Once / Compute Once / Source Once / Decision Once
[ENG-272-R001] Data Once: do not ask for known reliable data again unless verification is needed.
[ENG-272-R002] Compute Once: derived facts are reusable with version/provenance and invalidated on dependency change.
[ENG-272-R003] Source Once: preserve immutable originals and derive views instead of copying truth.
[ENG-272-R004] Decision Once: approved intent/plan propagates into downstream work without repetitive manual re-entry.
ENG-273 | ACTIVE | 273. Failure semantics for target architecture
[ENG-273-R001] If Patient State is stale, label it and permit source drill-down.
[ENG-273-R002] If Knowledge Engine is unavailable, preserve charting and deterministic core and suppress unsupported knowledge claims.
[ENG-273-R003] If AI is unavailable, preserve all deterministic/knowledge/closed-loop core workflows.
[ENG-273-R004] If obligation scheduler is degraded, expose operational incident and reconcile from authoritative due-state.
[ENG-273-R005] If graph projection fails, authoritative facts remain available.
ENG-274 | ACTIVE | 274. Safe-mode physician experience
[ENG-274-R001] Safe mode prioritizes patient identity, allergies/critical safety data, authoritative chart, medication list, critical results, obligations, basic documentation/prescribing where validated dependencies remain available and audit.
[ENG-274-R002] Nonessential AI, analytics and simulation can disappear without blocking care.
[ENG-274-R003] UI clearly distinguishes unavailable assistance from absence of clinical risk.
ENG-275 | ACTIVE | 275. Human-factors validation for target capabilities
[ENG-275-R001] Evaluate whether Goals, Hypotheses, Contradictions, Missing Evidence and Attention Budget improve decisions without increasing cognitive overload.
[ENG-275-R002] Measure time-to-state comprehension, information-not-found, unsafe interpretation, alert burden, interruptions and workaround creation.
[ENG-275-R003] Use synthetic 2,000-physician cohort for adversarial discovery followed by representative real-clinician validation for higher-risk functions.
ENG-276 | ACTIVE | 276. Target architecture threat-model expansion
[ENG-276-R001] Threat-model malicious/incorrect AI linking, graph poisoning, false provenance, patient-context confusion, obligation manipulation, preference leakage, plan-compiler privilege escalation and cross-tenant derived-state contamination.
[ENG-276-R002] Each new trust boundary receives STRIDE/abuse-case review appropriate to risk.
[ENG-276-R003] Security findings that can cause clinical harm enter the clinical hazard system as well.
ENG-277 | ACTIVE | 277. Target architecture privacy expansion
[ENG-277-R001] Intent, preferences, hypotheses and uncertainty may reveal highly sensitive context and require minimum-necessary access/egress.
[ENG-277-R002] AI context compiler selects fields by task/purpose allowlist.
[ENG-277-R003] Patient-facing portal never exposes clinician-only differential/hypothesis content by default without product/policy decision.
ENG-278 | ACTIVE | 278. Target architecture observability
[ENG-278-R001] Metrics include Patient State freshness, delta build latency, plan compile success/partial failure, expected-result overdue counts, orphan reconciliation, contradiction signal volume, attention budget, AI abstention and graph rebuild divergence.
[ENG-278-R002] Observability uses IDs/classes rather than raw PHI.
[ENG-278-R003] Every critical signal has owner and runbook.
ENG-279 | ACTIVE | 279. Target architecture performance budgets
[ENG-279-R001] Patient workspace must not require loading the entire longitudinal graph.
[ENG-279-R002] Use bounded projections, incremental read models, pagination and on-demand deep history.
[ENG-279-R003] Pre-visit Delta/State are precomputed or incrementally maintained where evidence shows latency benefit.
[ENG-279-R004] Long-chart and pathological-patient benchmarks become mandatory.
ENG-280 | ACTIVE | 280. Target architecture migration strategy
[ENG-280-R001] Introduce new primitives through expand-compatible schemas and backfill only facts that can be derived without inventing intent/meaning.
[ENG-280-R002] Legacy records with unknown intent/goal remain unknown; no synthetic historical certainty.
[ENG-280-R003] Shadow new Patient State/Delta projections before replacing existing read models.
ENG-281 | ACTIVE | 281. Product-gap promotion registry
[ENG-281-R001] Maintain PRODUCT-GAP IDs for physician-visible behavior introduced by this engineering expansion but absent from frozen V2.
[ENG-281-R002] At minimum: Clinical Intent, Goal Engine, Plan Compiler UX, Expected Result UX, Treatment Response, Hypothesis Workspace, Missing Evidence, Contradiction reconciliation, Uncertainty UX, Semantic Timeline, Patient Action Plan, Attention Budget and Care Team workflows.
[ENG-281-R003] No PRODUCT-GAP capability becomes unrestricted production behavior until product governance promotes/accepts it.
ENG-282 | ACTIVE | 282. Atomic traceability requirement
[ENG-282-R001] Every new capability receives Product ID (when promoted), ENG ID, invariant IDs, hazard IDs, API/schema refs, code owner, test IDs, telemetry IDs and evidence IDs.
[ENG-282-R002] Traceability must be queryable bidirectionally.
[ENG-282-R003] Orphan engineering requirement, orphan C4/C5 invariant and orphan safety test are release blockers.
ENG-283 | ACTIVE | 283. Target architecture executable-contract package
[ENG-283-R001] Create packages/contracts for opaque IDs, information states, quantities, provenance, ClinicalIntent, ClinicalGoal, ClinicalHypothesis, ClinicalDecision, ClinicalIntervention, ExpectedClinicalResult, TreatmentResponse, ClinicalEpisode, PatientPreference, ClinicalDelta and ClinicalActionReceipt.
[ENG-283-R002] Use TypeScript strict + runtime schema validation.
[ENG-283-R003] Contract package contains no UI/provider/database implementation.
ENG-284 | ACTIVE | 284. Target architecture state-machine package
[ENG-284-R001] Define explicit state machines for Goal, Hypothesis, Intervention, Expected Result, Treatment Response, Referral, Message and Obligation lifecycles.
[ENG-284-R002] Illegal transitions fail closed and are property/model tested.
[ENG-284-R003] State-machine version changes receive semantic diff.
ENG-285 | ACTIVE | 285. Target architecture graph contract
[ENG-285-R001] Define allowed node/edge types, directionality, cardinality constraints, temporal semantics, provenance requirements and bounded query rules.
[ENG-285-R002] Graph projection is reconstructable from authoritative domain facts/events.
[ENG-285-R003] Graph database adoption is optional and evidence-driven; logical graph model does not require a graph DB.
ENG-286 | ACTIVE | 286. Target architecture API contract
[ENG-286-R001] High-impact mutations use command-oriented APIs with patient/tenant binding, expected version/idempotency, explicit outcome union and action receipt.
[ENG-286-R002] Read APIs expose freshness/degraded/provenance metadata for computed views.
[ENG-286-R003] Batch APIs preserve per-item authorization and per-item outcomes.
ENG-287 | ACTIVE | 287. Target architecture database contract
[ENG-287-R001] Use normalized authoritative tables/events for new primitives plus derived read models where needed.
[ENG-287-R002] Foreign keys/constraints enforce tenant/patient ownership and valid relationships where feasible.
[ENG-287-R003] Do not persist AI prose as authoritative clinical state without explicit accepted object/state.
ENG-288 | ACTIVE | 288. Target architecture event contract
[ENG-288-R001] Events for goal, plan, result, response, obligation and correction are versioned, idempotent and carry tenant/patient/resource identity.
[ENG-288-R002] Consumers tolerate duplicate/reordered delivery and reconcile from authoritative state.
[ENG-288-R003] Events are integration facts, not authorization tokens.
ENG-289 | ACTIVE | 289. Target architecture test corpus
[ENG-289-R001] Extend Synthetic Patient Factory with goal/intent/response/contradiction/missing-evidence scenarios across life stages, multimorbidity, polypharmacy, incomplete imports and longitudinal corrections.
[ENG-289-R002] Add wrong-patient, stale-tab, concurrent-plan, duplicate-result, corrected-result, knowledge-update and AI-outage scenarios.
[ENG-289-R003] Maintain negative cases where the correct behavior is to say insufficient information/do nothing.
ENG-290 | ACTIVE | 290. Golden target-architecture journeys
[ENG-290-R001] Pre-visit: identify meaningful delta and pending obligations.
[ENG-290-R002] Encounter: capture intent/goals, reason through hypothesis/evidence/uncertainty and approve plan.
[ENG-290-R003] Plan compile: create prescription/order/monitoring/follow-up without re-entry.
[ENG-290-R004] Result loop: receive/correct/review/action/communicate/reassess.
[ENG-290-R005] Treatment loop: intervention → expected response → observed response → reassessment.
[ENG-290-R006] Referral loop: question → specialist response → reconciliation.
[ENG-290-R007] Patient loop: action plan → barrier/comprehension → routed follow-up.
ENG-291 | ACTIVE | 291. Golden target-architecture failure journeys
[ENG-291-R001] AI proposes wrong patient context; output discarded.
[ENG-291-R002] Plan compiler partially fails; no silent orphan work.
[ENG-291-R003] Expected result never arrives; obligation escalates.
[ENG-291-R004] Corrected result reverses prior interpretation; downstream state reopens.
[ENG-291-R005] Medication dependency changes; only affected checks update.
[ENG-291-R006] Contradictory facts remain unresolved; system abstains from false certainty.
[ENG-291-R007] Knowledge unavailable; deterministic core remains usable.
[ENG-291-R008] Clinician interrupted mid-plan; draft/recovery preserves context without committing.
ENG-292 | ACTIVE | 292. 30-pass circuit extension
[ENG-292-R001] All existing 30 adversarial passes are rerun against the new domain primitives.
[ENG-292-R002] Pass 02 explicitly attacks intent/goal/plan realism; Pass 05 information-state semantics; Pass 07 medication graph; Pass 08 goal/missing-evidence/contradiction knowledge; Pass 10 expected-result/response closure; Pass 16 new UI microstates; Pass 17 attention/cognitive load; Pass 28 synthetic physician workflows; Pass 29 cross-domain graph interactions.
[ENG-292-R003] Major fixes invalidate affected later passes.
ENG-293 | ACTIVE | 293. 150-expert ownership extension
[ENG-293-R001] Clinical Domain team owns Intent/Goal/Hypothesis/Decision primitives.
[ENG-293-R002] Medication/Orders/Results/Follow-up team owns Plan Compiler, Expected Result and Treatment Response integration.
[ENG-293-R003] Knowledge team owns computable goal/evidence/contradiction artifacts.
[ENG-293-R004] Frontend/Human Factors owns Attention Budget, Delta and semantic timeline experience.
[ENG-293-R005] AI team owns candidate extraction/proposal only within declared authority.
[ENG-293-R006] QA/SRE/Security maintain independent challenge.
ENG-294 | ACTIVE | 294. Clinical safety hazards for target architecture
[ENG-294-R001] Add hazards: false goal attainment, missing expected result, false contradiction resolution, AI-invented intent, incorrect episode grouping, inappropriate plan propagation, stale treatment response, preference mistaken for contraindication, hidden uncertainty and alert aggregation suppressing a critical signal.
[ENG-294-R002] Each hazard receives prevention/detection/containment/recovery and validation evidence.
ENG-295 | ACTIVE | 295. Regulatory/intended-use change gate
[ENG-295-R001] Each new CDS/AI function receives function-level intended-use review before production.
[ENG-295-R002] Capabilities that influence diagnosis/treatment more strongly may alter regulatory posture and cannot inherit a blanket classification from the rest of Medical OS.
[ENG-295-R003] Engineering specification does not claim regulatory exemption, certification or approval.
ENG-296 | ACTIVE | 296. Clinical knowledge source gate
[ENG-296-R001] No new medical threshold, dosing rule, diagnostic criterion, monitoring interval or recommendation is invented in engineering code/spec.
[ENG-296-R002] Such content enters through governed Knowledge/Algorithm pipelines with authoritative source, jurisdiction, effective date, clinical review and tests.
[ENG-296-R003] Examples in UI/test fixtures must be labeled synthetic unless sourced/validated.
ENG-297 | ACTIVE | 297. Target architecture definition of done
[ENG-297-R001] A capability is not done because UI/API exists.
[ENG-297-R002] DoD requires product authority or PRODUCT-GAP status, domain contract, state machine, information states, provenance, authz, audit, failure policy, reconciliation, migrations, tests, observability, performance budget, security review, clinical safety review, rollback/recovery and documentation.
[ENG-297-R003] C4/C5 additionally require clinical validation evidence appropriate to intended use.
ENG-298 | ACTIVE | 298. Target architecture release admission
[ENG-298-R001] No unresolved S0/S1.
[ENG-298-R002] No orphan C4/C5 requirement/invariant/test.
[ENG-298-R003] No silent unknown→normal/negative.
[ENG-298-R004] No AI high-impact authority escalation.
[ENG-298-R005] No unowned critical work.
[ENG-298-R006] No high-impact derived fact without version/provenance.
[ENG-298-R007] No Patient State/Graph divergence beyond defined reconciled tolerance.
[ENG-298-R008] No production activation of unresolved PRODUCT-GAP behavior.
ENG-299 | ACTIVE | 299. Architecture objective after this expansion
[ENG-299-R001] Medical OS shall preserve a computable representation of what is known, unknown, changing, intended, expected, decided, done, observed and still owed for a patient.
[ENG-299-R002] The platform shall prefer explicit state and closed loops over narrative inference.
[ENG-299-R003] The system shall use the least probabilistic mechanism capable of solving the problem correctly.
[ENG-299-R004] The physician remains final authority for clinically consequential judgment under the defined intended use.
ENG-300 | ACTIVE | 300. V2.1.1 target-architecture freeze marker
[ENG-300-R001] Sections 212–300 form the Ultra-Senior Target Architecture Expansion.
[ENG-300-R002] They augment, not erase, the prior Full-Fidelity V2.1.1 engineering baseline and Companion-parity annex.
[ENG-300-R003] Next required governance action is promotion of PRODUCT-GAP items into the product specification, followed by bidirectional traceability and executable contracts.
[ENG-300-R004] Further prose expansion should be justified by a discovered requirement gap; otherwise work moves to schemas, state machines, ADRs, threat models, tests and code.

V2.1.1 — FORMAL CLINICAL ARCHITECTURE EXPANSION
[ENG-301-R001] This expansion hardens the Ultra-Senior Target Architecture into a formal clinical architecture. Its purpose is to reduce semantic ambiguity, make safety properties executable, and establish replay, reconciliation, impact analysis, bounded AI authority and clinically truthful UI contracts. It augments the prior V2.1.1 baseline; physician-visible PRODUCT-GAP behavior remains gated by product governance.
ENG-302 | ACTIVE | 302. Clinical Semantic Kernel
[ENG-302-R001] Define a minimal canonical kernel: ClinicalFact, ClinicalAssertion, ClinicalObservation, ClinicalProblem, ClinicalHypothesis, ClinicalEvidence, ClinicalUncertainty, ClinicalIntent, ClinicalGoal, ClinicalDecision, ClinicalIntervention, ExpectedResult, ObservedResult, TreatmentResponse, ClinicalObligation, ClinicalPreference, ClinicalEpisode, ClinicalRisk, ClinicalContradiction and ClinicalProvenance.
[ENG-302-R002] Every clinically meaningful primitive declares identity, tenant, patient, semantic type, information state, verification state, source, provenance, effective time, recorded time, actor, version, supersession, relationships, authorization class and sensitivity where applicable.
[ENG-302-R003] Domain teams MUST extend the kernel rather than invent parallel meanings for fact, evidence, result, state, pending or verification.
ENG-303 | ACTIVE | 303. Clinical Epistemology / Truth Model
[ENG-303-R001] Medical OS MUST distinguish what was reported, imported, extracted, inferred, considered, verified, diagnosed and accepted.
[ENG-303-R002] Canonical flow: Source → Assertion → Verification → Clinical Interpretation → Accepted Fact/Problem/Hypothesis.
[ENG-303-R003] Patient statement, external document, AI extraction, clinician hypothesis and confirmed diagnosis are never semantically interchangeable.
[ENG-303-R004] Source authority and recency may influence reconciliation but cannot erase original provenance.
ENG-304 | ACTIVE | 304. Fact–Derived Fact–Inference–Recommendation–Decision separation
[ENG-304-R001] Define type-level separation between observed/verified fact, deterministic derived fact, probabilistic inference, knowledge recommendation, AI suggestion, physician decision and committed clinical action.
[ENG-304-R002] No implicit conversion may promote a lower-authority object into a higher-authority object.
[ENG-304-R003] UI, API and audit MUST preserve the authority class so an AI suggestion cannot visually or structurally masquerade as a verified result.
ENG-305 | ACTIVE | 305. Clinical relationship semantics and causal guardrails
[ENG-305-R001] Clinical graph edges MUST use explicit semantics such as supports, contradicts, temporally_follows, associated_with, clinician_attributed_to, targeted_by and expected_to_affect.
[ENG-305-R002] Temporal sequence does not imply causality.
[ENG-305-R003] Causal claims require an explicitly qualified source/model/clinician attribution and remain distinguishable from association.
ENG-306 | ACTIVE | 306. State Machine Constitution
[ENG-306-R001] All clinically consequential lifecycle states are closed enumerations with explicit transitions, actors, guards, side effects and illegal-transition behavior.
[ENG-306-R002] Required state machines include Result, Medication, Goal, Hypothesis, Referral, Obligation, Decision/Plan, Document, Message and Appointment where applicable.
[ENG-306-R003] State strings MUST NOT be free-form database values for safety-critical lifecycles.
[ENG-306-R004] State-machine versions receive semantic diff and migration review.
ENG-307 | ACTIVE | 307. Result Lifecycle 2.0
[ENG-307-R001] Canonical result lifecycle distinguishes EXPECTED, ORDERED, RECEIVED, VERIFIED, REVIEWED, ACTION_REQUIRED, ACTIONED, PATIENT_INFORMED and CLOSED, with governed branches for CORRECTED, CANCELLED, UNMATCHED, FAILED, OVERDUE and ESCALATED.
[ENG-307-R002] Received ≠ verified ≠ reviewed ≠ actioned ≠ patient informed ≠ closed.
[ENG-307-R003] Corrected results can invalidate downstream derived state and reopen review/action obligations.
ENG-308 | ACTIVE | 308. Zero Lost Follow-Up formal invariant
[ENG-308-R001] For every applicable clinically relevant future action, exactly one accountable lifecycle MUST remain observable until completed, cancelled-with-reason, superseded or explicitly transferred.
[ENG-308-R002] Ownerless, silently expired or queue-lost critical work is an invariant violation.
[ENG-308-R003] An independent reconciler MUST detect violations from authoritative state rather than trusting workflow queues.
ENG-309 | ACTIVE | 309. Clinical Invariant Registry
[ENG-309-R001] Create a machine-queryable registry for critical invariants with ID, scope, severity, prevention, database/domain guard, test oracle, property test, reconciliation check, telemetry, incident classification and owner.
[ENG-309-R002] Seed invariants include tenant isolation, patient binding, signed immutability, result closure, obligation ownership, AI non-promotion, unit safety, unknown-state preservation and knowledge-version traceability.
[ENG-309-R003] C4/C5 behavior without mapped invariant/test is a release blocker.
ENG-310 | ACTIVE | 310. Formal verification escalation policy
[ENG-310-R001] Apply formal/model-based/property verification selectively to high-value invariants rather than attempting formal proof of the entire platform.
[ENG-310-R002] Priority targets: tenant isolation, patient binding, signed records, result/obligation lifecycles, unit/dose arithmetic, Plan Compiler commit semantics, corrected-result propagation, idempotency and critical authorization transitions.
[ENG-310-R003] Formal verification evidence supplements but does not replace clinical validation.
ENG-311 | ACTIVE | 311. Global Clinical Reconciliation Engine
[ENG-311-R001] Implement continuous and scheduled reconciliation across results, orders, goals, interventions, monitoring, referrals, obligations, documents, projections, audit and external side effects.
[ENG-311-R002] Reconciliation detects orphan, stale, divergent and impossible states and creates repair work without inventing clinical facts.
[ENG-311-R003] Architecture principle: Prevention + Detection + Reconciliation + Recovery.
ENG-312 | ACTIVE | 312. Clinical Replay
[ENG-312-R001] Medical OS MUST be able to reconstruct why a clinically consequential state/recommendation was shown at a historical time.
[ENG-312-R002] Replay inputs include facts available at T, knowledge/algorithm/terminology versions, configuration, authorization context, Patient State projection, application/schema release and AI task/model configuration when used.
[ENG-312-R003] Replay is deterministic where the original function was deterministic; nondeterministic AI outputs preserve original output/config and are not regenerated as historical truth.
ENG-313 | ACTIVE | 313. Patient Impact Query
[ENG-313-R001] Provide governed reverse-trace queries from defective/superseded algorithm, knowledge artifact, terminology release, parser, AI task/model, migration or software release to affected executions, patients, displayed outputs and downstream actions where traceability exists.
[ENG-313-R002] Impact analysis is purpose/authorization scoped and privacy-preserving.
[ENG-313-R003] High-impact recalls/defects MUST have an operational runbook using this capability.
ENG-314 | ACTIVE | 314. Action-Level Clinical BOM
[ENG-314-R001] Extend Clinical BOM from release level to clinically consequential computation/action receipts where justified.
[ENG-314-R002] Record application/schema, algorithm, knowledge pack, terminology, policy, configuration and AI task/model versions contributing to an action.
[ENG-314-R003] Clinical BOM metadata supports replay, impact analysis and incident response; it is not necessarily physician-facing.
ENG-315 | ACTIVE | 315. Clinical Policy Engine separation
[ENG-315-R001] Separate evidence/medical knowledge from institutional policy and jurisdictional/legal constraints.
[ENG-315-R002] Decision context may combine Evidence Recommendation, Institutional Policy, Patient Preference/Constraint and Legal/Regulatory Constraint without falsely presenting them as one authority.
[ENG-315-R003] Policy artifacts are versioned, scoped, explainable and auditable.
ENG-316 | ACTIVE | 316. Semantic Interoperability Firewall
[ENG-316-R001] External FHIR/HL7/DICOM/document/API data enters through validation, identity resolution, terminology normalization, unit validation, provenance, trust classification and semantic mapping before becoming Medical OS domain data.
[ENG-316-R002] External payloads are untrusted input; interoperability adapters cannot bypass domain invariants or authorization.
[ENG-316-R003] FHIR remains an interoperability boundary, not the internal clinical ontology.
ENG-317 | ACTIVE | 317. Clinical Data Trust Dimensions
[ENG-317-R001] Represent trust as explainable dimensions rather than a single opaque score: source, verification, recency, identity match, terminology resolution, unit verification, completeness and conflict state.
[ENG-317-R002] Do not fabricate a numerical trust percentage without a validated model.
[ENG-317-R003] Trust dimensions influence display/reconciliation/applicability but never erase provenance.
ENG-318 | ACTIVE | 318. Pediatric Clinical Context hardening
[ENG-318-R001] Pediatrics MUST model gestational/corrected age where applicable, weight/height trajectory, BMI percentile where appropriate, BSA, development, vaccination, guardian/consent and medication dose basis.
[ENG-318-R002] Every weight-dependent calculation preserves weight_used, weight_date, weight_source and verification state.
[ENG-318-R003] Latest weight is not automatically the correct dose weight.
[ENG-318-R004] Unknown/ambiguous concentration, route, unit or weight can force NOT_COMPUTABLE.
ENG-319 | ACTIVE | 319. Context Applicability Framework
[ENG-319-R001] Define typed clinical contexts such as PediatricContext, PregnancyContext, GeriatricContext, RenalContext, HepaticContext, AnticoagulationContext and ImmunocompromisedContext.
[ENG-319-R002] Knowledge/algorithms declare required/optional/exclusion contexts rather than scattering hard-coded conditionals.
[ENG-319-R003] Context activation has provenance and effective time.
ENG-320 | ACTIVE | 320. Safety Envelope Contract
[ENG-320-R001] Every clinically consequential computation declares supported inputs/context, unsupported conditions, preconditions, exclusions, output semantics and explicit failure/abstention states.
[ENG-320-R002] Unsupported/ambiguous input MUST fail as NOT_COMPUTABLE, INSUFFICIENT_DATA, CONFLICTING_DATA or another typed state rather than best-effort clinical guessing.
[ENG-320-R003] Safety envelope changes require versioning and validation.
ENG-321 | ACTIVE | 321. AI Task Card Registry
[ENG-321-R001] Replace the concept of one generic Medical OS AI with a registry of narrow AI tasks.
[ENG-321-R002] Each AI Task Card defines task ID/version, purpose, input allowlist, output schema, permitted actions, prohibited actions, evidence requirements, abstention policy, risk class, eval suite, owner and model/config compatibility.
[ENG-321-R003] Direct model SDK usage outside the governed AI Gateway remains prohibited.
ENG-322 | ACTIVE | 322. AI Evidence Coverage
[ENG-322-R001] For AI outputs containing clinical claims, track claim-level evidence coverage where technically feasible: directly supported, inferential, unsupported/unknown.
[ENG-322-R002] Unsupported high-impact claims MUST be suppressed, marked or routed to verification according to task policy.
[ENG-322-R003] A global confidence score cannot substitute for claim-level grounding.
ENG-323 | ACTIVE | 323. AI independent verification pattern
[ENG-323-R001] For selected extraction/normalization tasks, permit extractor → independent verifier → deterministic schema validator → conflict routing.
[ENG-323-R002] Multiple models do not automatically make an output clinically true; correlated-error risk is assessed.
[ENG-323-R003] Verifier disagreement produces abstention/human review rather than majority-vote clinical authority.
ENG-324 | ACTIVE | 324. UI Truthfulness Contract
[ENG-324-R001] Design System MUST visually distinguish observed/verified, patient-reported, imported, derived, suggested, unverified, pending and conflicting information.
[ENG-324-R002] AI suggestions MUST NOT share the same visual authority as verified clinical results/physician decisions.
[ENG-324-R003] Semantic status cannot depend on color alone; accessibility and text/icon cues are required.
ENG-325 | ACTIVE | 325. Clinical Progressive Disclosure
[ENG-325-R001] High-density clinical information follows progressive disclosure: priority signal → reason → evidence/context → provenance/version.
[ENG-325-R002] Default view minimizes cognitive burden while preserving rapid drill-down.
[ENG-325-R003] Safety-critical context cannot be hidden behind an interaction that routinely prevents recognition.
ENG-326 | ACTIVE | 326. Why Is This Here? universal explanation contract
[ENG-326-R001] Any high-impact signal/recommendation/derived fact MUST support a trace explaining triggering data, change/baseline, affected object, rule/algorithm/knowledge version, source and missing/conflicting inputs.
[ENG-326-R002] Prefer deterministic execution traces; LLMs may translate traces but not invent rationale.
ENG-327 | ACTIVE | 327. Clinical Density Profiles
[ENG-327-R001] Support governed presentation profiles such as FOCUSED, STANDARD and DENSE without changing underlying clinical meaning or safety semantics.
[ENG-327-R002] Density preference cannot suppress mandatory critical safety information.
[ENG-327-R003] Do not create hundreds of per-field customization switches that fragment validation.
ENG-328 | ACTIVE | 328. Digital Twin Clinic CI integration
[ENG-328-R001] Make the synthetic Digital Twin Clinic part of continuous verification.
[ENG-328-R002] High-risk PRs execute targeted synthetic journeys; nightly suites run broader longitudinal simulations; release candidates run accelerated multi-month/year scenarios.
[ENG-328-R003] Faults include network partitions, duplicate/reordered events, corrected results, staff turnover, patient merge, knowledge updates, AI outage, migration, time-zone/date boundaries and pathological long charts.
ENG-329 | ACTIVE | 329. Adversarial Synthetic Clinician Agents
[ENG-329-R001] Model diverse synthetic clinician interaction patterns: high-volume, novice/expert, interruption-heavy, multi-tab, keyboard-only, mobile, alert-dismissive, copy-forward, idle-session, double-submit and incomplete-data workflows.
[ENG-329-R002] The purpose is defect discovery, not simulated proof of usability or clinical validity.
[ENG-329-R003] Real clinician validation remains required where risk/intended use demands it.
ENG-330 | ACTIVE | 330. Clinical Safety Red Team
[ENG-330-R001] Maintain an independent red-team program targeting wrong patient, wrong dose/unit, false certainty, lost result, misleading AI, stale knowledge, contradictory chart, unsafe override, alert fatigue and workflow dead ends.
[ENG-330-R002] Security defects capable of clinical harm are jointly triaged with clinical safety.
[ENG-330-R003] Red-team findings feed hazard registry and regression corpus.
ENG-331 | ACTIVE | 331. Complexity Budget / Feature Admission
[ENG-331-R001] Every material feature must justify clinical value, cognitive-load reduction, safety value and operational value against implementation complexity, maintenance burden and new failure modes.
[ENG-331-R002] Features that add steps/screens/fields without sufficient value should be rejected or redesigned.
[ENG-331-R003] Complexity budget is an architecture-governance tool, not a numerical clinical score.
ENG-332 | ACTIVE | 332. Executable Documentation Transition
[ENG-332-R001] After PRODUCT-GAP promotion and final normalization, master documents SHOULD be frozen as constitutions and work should move into executable repository artifacts.
[ENG-332-R002] Required repository families include requirements, domain contracts, state machines, invariants, hazards, ADRs, threat models, algorithms, knowledge, AI tasks, APIs, data, reconciliation, SRE and validation.
[ENG-332-R003] Further prose expansion requires a demonstrated specification gap.
ENG-333 | ACTIVE | 333. Clinical Kernel package boundary
[ENG-333-R001] Create packages/clinical-kernel containing canonical semantic types and authority classes with no UI, database-provider, model-provider or transport dependencies.
[ENG-333-R002] Other clinical packages depend inward on the kernel; the kernel cannot depend on specialty packs.
[ENG-333-R003] Breaking semantic changes require explicit migration and compatibility review.
ENG-334 | ACTIVE | 334. Truth Model package boundary
[ENG-334-R001] Create typed assertion/source/verification/interpretation contracts and conversion functions requiring explicit authority transitions.
[ENG-334-R002] Prohibit generic cast/promote helpers that bypass verification.
[ENG-334-R003] Imported and AI-extracted assertions remain reconstructable after clinician acceptance.
ENG-335 | ACTIVE | 335. Invariant-as-Code package
[ENG-335-R001] Create a registry that binds invariant IDs to executable checks/tests/telemetry and owning modules.
[ENG-335-R002] CI verifies that required critical invariants are referenced by affected high-risk modules.
[ENG-335-R003] Runtime reconciliation may execute selected invariant checks over authoritative state.
ENG-336 | ACTIVE | 336. Replay and Impact service boundary
[ENG-336-R001] Replay/Impact services are read-oriented privileged capabilities with strict purpose authorization and comprehensive audit.
[ENG-336-R002] They consume immutable/versioned execution metadata and cannot mutate historical clinical truth.
[ENG-336-R003] Large impact queries use bounded, resumable jobs and privacy-safe exports.
ENG-337 | ACTIVE | 337. Reconciliation ownership model
[ENG-337-R001] Every asynchronous closed loop declares authoritative source, expected derived/side-effect state, reconciliation query, repair action, owner and escalation.
[ENG-337-R002] Queue consumer success is not proof of business completion.
[ENG-337-R003] Reconciliation jobs are idempotent and observable.
ENG-338 | ACTIVE | 338. Clinical Computation Safety Envelope schema
[ENG-338-R001] Algorithm manifests gain supportedContexts, unsupportedContexts, requiredInputs, ambiguityRules, abstentionStates, outputAuthority, clinicalRiskClass and validationEvidence references.
[ENG-338-R002] Runtime validates the safety envelope before executing or presenting a result.
[ENG-338-R003] Safety envelope violation is an explicit typed result.
ENG-339 | ACTIVE | 339. AI Task Card schema
[ENG-339-R001] AI task manifests gain inputPurpose, minimumNecessaryFields, outputSchema, claimGroundingPolicy, allowedTools, prohibitedMutations, patientBindingPolicy, stalenessPolicy, abstentionPolicy, evalSuite, riskClass, owner and killSwitch.
[ENG-339-R002] Task configuration/model changes require compatibility/eval gates based on risk.
ENG-340 | ACTIVE | 340. UI Semantic Status tokens
[ENG-340-R001] Design System exposes semantic tokens/components for VERIFIED, PATIENT_REPORTED, IMPORTED, DERIVED, SUGGESTED, UNVERIFIED, PENDING, CONFLICTING and DEGRADED.
[ENG-340-R002] Tokens are semantic contracts, not branding colors.
[ENG-340-R003] Snapshot/accessibility tests verify status remains distinguishable across themes and density profiles.
ENG-341 | ACTIVE | 341. Pediatric Dose Computation receipt
[ENG-341-R001] Every weight-dependent medication calculation records drug/formulation/concentration/route, weight_used, weight_date/source, dose basis, algorithm/knowledge version, calculated dose, capped dose when applicable, administration volume and warnings/abstention.
[ENG-341-R002] Clinical content such as actual dose ranges/maxima remains governed knowledge and is not defined by this architecture section.
ENG-342 | ACTIVE | 342. Corrected Result propagation DAG
[ENG-342-R001] Derived facts, goals, treatment responses, alerts and obligations depending on a result are represented in a dependency graph sufficient for invalidation/recomputation.
[ENG-342-R002] Correction creates a new authoritative version and invalidates affected derived nodes without deleting historical outputs.
[ENG-342-R003] Prior physician decisions remain historically reconstructable with the evidence available at decision time.
ENG-343 | ACTIVE | 343. Knowledge/Policy conflict representation
[ENG-343-R001] When evidence guidance, institutional policy, jurisdictional constraint or patient preference conflict, Medical OS represents the conflict explicitly.
[ENG-343-R002] Conflict resolution authority is defined by policy/intended use; AI cannot silently harmonize competing authorities.
[ENG-343-R003] Explanation identifies which authority drove the final constraint/recommendation.
ENG-344 | ACTIVE | 344. Clinical Context invalidation
[ENG-344-R001] Changes to age band, pregnancy state, renal/hepatic state, weight or other validated context dependencies invalidate affected computations/knowledge applicability.
[ENG-344-R002] Dependency invalidation is incremental and bounded.
[ENG-344-R003] Unrelated alerts/recommendations are not regenerated merely because one context changed.
ENG-345 | ACTIVE | 345. Epistemic UI safety tests
[ENG-345-R001] Human-factors tests MUST verify clinicians can distinguish verified fact, patient report, imported data, AI suggestion, hypothesis, recommendation and physician decision under time pressure.
[ENG-345-R002] Misclassification of authority class is a safety defect.
[ENG-345-R003] Test across desktop, tablet/mobile and supported accessibility modes.
ENG-346 | ACTIVE | 346. Formal target architecture acceptance gate
[ENG-346-R001] Before declaring this architecture implemented: semantic kernel has one canonical definition per primitive; authority transitions are explicit; critical lifecycles are state-machine governed; critical invariants are executable; closed loops reconcile; replay and impact analysis work for selected high-risk paths; AI tasks are bounded; UI truthfulness is validated; safety envelopes fail explicitly.
[ENG-346-R002] Zero unresolved S0/S1 defects remains mandatory.
[ENG-346-R003] Production readiness still requires product authority, clinical validation, security/privacy evidence, operational readiness and applicable regulatory review.
ENG-347 | ACTIVE | 347. V2.1.1 Formal Clinical Architecture freeze marker
[ENG-347-R001] Sections 302–347 form the Formal Clinical Architecture Expansion.
[ENG-347-R002] They build on sections 212–301 and the prior V2.1.1/Companion parity body.
[ENG-347-R003] The next documentation action is to update the Agent Execution Companion to these requirements and then perform bidirectional normalization rather than continuing uncontrolled narrative expansion.

ENG-NORM | ACTIVE | Engineering Authority Normalization Control
This block normalizes the V2.1.1 engineering authority namespace. It adds no new physician-visible product behavior.
ENG-xxx identifies a canonical engineering architecture section.
ENG-xxx-Ryyy identifies an atomic normative engineering statement within that section.
ACTIVE means current engineering authority.
FUTURE means an explicitly future architecture boundary and is not release authority until promoted through governance.
ANNEX-PARITY/HISTORICAL material is retained for provenance but is not a canonical requirement namespace.
Bare numeric references inside the historical parity annex must never be used as code/test requirement IDs.
Engineering requirements may implement or constrain product authority, but may not silently redefine ACTIVE PROD-* behavior.
When an engineering requirement has no direct physician-visible PROD-* parent, its authority must be an explicit architecture/security/safety/compliance authority rather than an invented product requirement.
Executable traceability must use stable IDs: PROD-* → ENG-* → EXEC-* → invariant/hazard/test/evidence.
