ENG-FINAL | ACTIVE | Engineering Architecture Authority Finalization Gate
Purpose: close the engineering-document findings from the normalized triple audit without inventing product mappings or treating semantic similarity as authority. This governance block adds no physician-visible product behavior.
ENG-000 through ENG-348 are the canonical V2.1.1 engineering architecture section identifiers; atomic engineering requirements use ENG-xxx-Ryyy.
ENG identifiers are immutable after this finalization. Future changes require governed new IDs or explicit supersession; silent renumbering is prohibited.
ANNEX-PARITY remains HISTORICAL / NON-CANONICAL. Its internal numeric headings are provenance evidence and must never be used as active engineering requirement IDs.
An ACTIVE ENG requirement must have explicit authority before release: either one or more ACTIVE PROD-* requirements or a declared architectural/security/safety/compliance authority class.
Semantic similarity, inherited context, adjacent prose, or model inference are never sufficient authority for PROD→ENG or ENG→EXEC traceability.
An ACTIVE ENG requirement without explicit downstream execution mapping is not deleted or guessed; it is TRACEABILITY-PENDING and blocks release of the affected behavior until the executable registry resolves it.
ENG-252 and ENG-255 remain FUTURE and cannot satisfy ACTIVE release requirements or become release-active because code, EXEC prose, prototypes, tests or scaffolds exist.
Engineering may constrain implementation to preserve safety/security/reliability, but it may not silently redefine physician-visible ACTIVE PROD behavior.
For C4/C5 or otherwise clinically consequential behavior, engineering authority requires explicit invariant, failure semantics, test oracle, reconciliation where asynchronous, provenance/version traceability, and human authority where applicable.
Critical state transitions must be closed state machines; unknown/missing/unsupported states must not collapse into normal/negative/success.
Critical asynchronous loops require an accountable reconciliation mechanism and recovery path independent of best-effort queue delivery.
High-impact AI behavior requires bounded task authority, typed inputs/outputs, abstention/failure behavior, evidence/grounding requirements where applicable, evaluation ownership and kill-switch capability.
Conflicting PROD/ENG/EXEC authority is BLOCKED: SPEC_CONFLICT until governance resolves the conflict.
The executable traceability registry is the machine authority for cross-document mappings; this master remains the architecture authority, not the mapping database.

Engineering authority invariants
EAI-001 — Every canonical engineering section has exactly one stable ENG section ID.
EAI-002 — Every atomic ENG requirement has exactly one canonical ENG parent.
EAI-003 — ANNEX-PARITY content cannot be release authority.
EAI-004 — ACTIVE ENG without explicit product/architectural authority cannot become release-active behavior.
EAI-005 — ACTIVE ENG without explicit execution mapping is TRACEABILITY-PENDING and cannot be assumed covered.
EAI-006 — FUTURE ENG cannot satisfy ACTIVE release requirements.
EAI-007 — C4/C5 behavior cannot release without executable invariant and test oracle.
EAI-008 — Critical asynchronous lifecycle cannot release without reconciliation and recovery semantics.
EAI-009 — High-impact AI cannot release without bounded task contract and human-authority boundary.
EAI-010 — Unknown/missing/unsupported information cannot be coerced into normal/negative/success.
EAI-011 — Cross-authority conflict blocks implementation until explicitly resolved.
EAI-012 — Cross-document mappings must be explicit and machine-readable; semantic similarity is not evidence.
Engineering architecture freeze declaration
ENGINEERING ARCHITECTURE AUTHORITY FREEZE: ENG-000 through ENG-348 are the finalized V2.1.1 architecture baseline. ENG-252 and ENG-255 remain FUTURE. ANNEX-PARITY remains HISTORICAL / NON-CANONICAL. This freeze does not claim code completion, clinical validation, security validation, regulatory certification, operational readiness or production release. Those require executable traceability, invariants, tests, reconciliation, hazard controls and release evidence.
